# Feature status

[한국어](features.ko.md)

Validation applies to the stated implementation only. Passing tests does not mean a change was released.

Host diagnostic input (`input.pointer`, `input.key`) must reach the application through the same dispatch as a person's mouse and keyboard. It does not yet: keys go to `-[NSWindow sendEvent:]` and skip the menu key equivalents that `-[NSApplication sendEvent:]` runs, and pointer presses, drags, and releases call the hit view directly. This is a defect of the diagnostic input, recorded as V5-45.

## Active implementation checklist

This is the canonical task checklist; the Korean file is its translation, not another work queue. `[ ]` means waiting, `[~]` means active, `[o]` means the stated acceptance criteria have current evidence, and `[!]` records a temporary bypass. Historical passes below do not validate later changes. The [verification contract](spec/verification.md) defines the gates.

Completed entries and their scoped evidence are historical records; follow-up items identify later corrections without changing the original evidence.


- [o] V5-118 — P0, highest priority: Add the card fullscreen toggle immediately before close X. The selected content card fills the work area; the same control restores the arrangement, without OS fullscreen or saved-layout mutation. Declare core.card.fullscreen command/DOM and core.grid fullscreen status. Preserve tabs, section state and live sibling surfaces. Red: missing view/command; native siblings remained visible; V10 misclassified retained invisible frames; invalid tab moves restored fullscreen before rejection; settings changes reused previous sidebar insets (190-point V7c); zero-area slots displayed retained native frames. Green: capture target insets with the actual production layout, remove the old inference path, and hide hidden/zero-area slots without disposal. Library 314/314, workbench 155/155, owning tracked repeats, six final rebuilt-host checks repeated three times (18 checks), and eight existing sidebar checks pass on macOS 26.6.2 arm64. Recorded native icon clicks measure card edges in fullscreen and restore; checks cover exact geometry, unchanged OS frame, runtime binding audit, live surfaces, resize, invalid input, selection restoration, both setting states and every intermediate verification notification. Final-setting measurements reserve 190+2 points: card/native widths 1186/994 fullscreen and 984/792 restored. Preserve failed preparation runs (1005), a failed capture stream with zero frames, an input refusal (1007), and an attempted repeat after applications had exited; none count as Green. The same unmodified final target subsequently passes three complete repeats. Final Wails PID 32491, Tauri PID 32539, started 2026-09-30T13:07:46Z/13:07:47Z; SHA-256 1f81071b6fb1993e157029cc97cd7ef8fab9690f27dd634f48801830d1ce3361 / 64c06d14f62fbeeca28df5aa929066565abdee92f6ec0bc59c526b361aac7ce4. Verified and committed as `d130d192`. Final shared gates, user-release application, window-sidebar ownership and orientation remain separate pending work.

  - [o] G2-3-5-1 — P1: Reject a `null` page exposure list in the Wails host instead of crashing it. `json.Unmarshal` decodes `null` into a nil map without an error, and the host then assigned host entries into that map. Red on 2026-09-23: `TestExposureListRejectsANonObjectPageList` extended to `[]`, `null`, `3`, and `"list"` stopped the test process with `panic: assignment to entry in nil map`. Green: `exposureList` rejects a nil list with -32603, the same code as the Tauri host; the Go tests pass in both configurations.
  - [o] G2-3-6 — P1: Wails (Go) host: add tests for the image cases `images.envelope.refusal-echoes-name-and-sequence`, `images.envelope.present-carries-nonce-scale-generation-raster`, `images.transfer.new-generation-invalidates-queued-old-frame`, `images.wait.successful-handle-replies-consumed`, `images.wait.newer-sequence-rearms-wait`, `images.present.rejects-frame-detached-during-main-thread`, and `images.present.missing-native-surface-requests-reconfiguration`. Acceptance: the host runs a passing test for each listed case, each new test fails before the behavior or assertion it checks is present, and `make host-contract-check` no longer lists these cases. Done on 2026-09-23. Writing the tests found three Wails behaviors that differed from the Tauri host: (1) `BeginGeneration` only advanced the number, so a queued frame of the old generation was still presented after a new generation began (Red: `TestBeginningANewGenerationInvalidatesAQueuedOldFrame` got a presentation); it now moves the surface's images to the new generation and clears their configuration, as `begin_generation` does. (2) A frame whose surface images were removed before the main-thread work was reported as a `notAttached` presentation failure; `presentationReason` now maps it to `stale`. (3) `PresentImage` returned only a boolean, so a `notFound` rejection became `presentFailed` and no fresh raster was requested; it now returns the native reason from `sp_region_last_error`, `HandleEnvelope` takes `onMain func(func() error) error` and a `recoverFrame` callback, and `notFound` refreshes the image rasters, as in the Tauri host. Running `browser.test.mjs` then `controls.test.mjs` alone reproduces neither the cascade on the current build nor on the build before `fbc8d554`, so the change of `fbc8d554` shows no harm; reproduce the cascade with a tracked sequence before correcting it. Diagnosis (2026-10-01): a Wails-only full run without tracing reproduces 15 `fresh()` shell-readiness timeouts in which the shell surface holds every plugin entry but not the earlier-registered `status core.surface.document`; with `SOKSAK_PERFORMANCE_TRACE=1` the same run has none. Wails v3 delivers `WebViewDidCommitNavigation` through a buffered channel and runs each listener in its own goroutine, so the host's reload work (`ready=false`, abandoning main-page requests, `reloadSurfaceDocuments`, `surfacesClosed`) can run after the replacement page has registered and attached; the page's `closed` handler removes every registration of that surface ID, and a late document cleanup explains `already attached`. Tauri performs the same work synchronously in `PageLoadEvent::Started`. Plan: the main page calls a new host method `pageStarted` before it registers or mounts anything, and both hosts perform the reload work there instead of in navigation callbacks; a diagnostic-only `diagnostics.navigation.delay` delays the hosts' navigation callbacks to reproduce the late callback deterministically for owning Red. `e2e/app.mjs` gains the strict `SOKSAK_PERFORMANCE_TRACE` switch and appends the registration timeline to a traced shell-readiness failure. This item covers V5-116-4-2-2. Correction: the main page calls `pageStarted` before it registers or mounts anything, both hosts perform the reload work there, and their navigation callbacks only refresh the WebKit child record; `diagnostics.navigation.delay` (diagnostic builds) delays those callbacks. Red on the unchanged Wails host: the new check `a late main-page navigation callback keeps the replacement page's registrations and documents` fails with `the late navigation callback marked the replacement page not ready`, and later checks on the same host then fail in preparation; Tauri passes because it performs the work before the page starts. Green: the same check and the existing registration lifecycle check pass 12/12 in three runs on both rebuilt hosts, `pnpm test`, host parity, exposure, boundaries, fallbacks and Rust format pass. The untraced Wails-only full window suite now ends with 127 passes and 29 failures of 157 (before: 112/43) with no shell-readiness timeout (before: 15), no `stale composition revision` (before: 3) and no `already attached`; the remaining failures belong to G1.4-14-2 to -4 (macOS 26.6.2 arm64).

After the change, both rebuilt applications answered in all 5 runs each as soon as the file appeared, with the `main` window at `ready: false`. A tracked 18-width sweep on the pre-fix Tauri build reproduced failures beginning at 1229pt: settings failed at 1229pt, theme and settings at 1276pt, left/right/theme/settings at 1323pt, and all five at 1370–1512pt. At failures, `host.hit` returned native `TaoView` across the full content frame even though the main WebView frame covered that content. The Tauri-like Red regression sets the initial WebView autoresizing mask to `NSViewNotSizable`, expands its parent from 800pt to 1200pt, and finds the compositor still 800pt wide with x=1100 hitting `NSView`. Green sets the wrapper compositor to resize with its parent; it becomes 1200pt wide and x=1100 hits `WKWebView`. The first post-fix runtime attempt right-aligned Tauri at x=312 and then enlarged it beyond the display; synthetic clicks at the widest samples activated the app, invalidating that run even though each button effect occurred. The tracked sweep now moves the window to the visible screen origin and asserts that every measured frame stays inside the display. On current debug builds, both Tauri and Wails pass all five native chrome clicks at 18 widths from 756pt through 1512pt; every click hits the main WebView and changes the expected state, while both apps remain inactive and non-key. The maximum-width captures contain 20 Tauri frames with an 11.59ms longest gap and 24 Wails frames with a 22.83ms longest gap. The first `make native-test` attempt exposed a stale verifier path: sidecar builds write `sidecars/vt-alacritty/build/soksak-vt-alacritty`, but the target invoked a missing `target/debug` binary and waited 60s without reporting the spawn error. A split request could therefore terminate the host before its presentation error was returned, leaving the visible request at `1005` or `loading`. Green routes endpoint and asynchronous split-path root-WebView lookups through the main-thread executor and adds an observable repeated endpoint split regression with per-case START/PASS output. F8-3 recorded on 2026-09-23 that rebuilt Wails removed its endpoint after `host.quit`, but the 5s shutdown observer still timed out; the failure was not registered, and F2 remains complete for its earlier scope. Acceptance: reproduce with measured timing on the current Wails build, identify the missing or late completion signal, correct it, and pass `e2e/normal-shutdown.mjs` on both rebuilt hosts without increasing its limit. Done on 2026-09-23: the defect was in the observer, not in Wails. On the rebuilt Wails host `e2e/normal-shutdown.mjs` exceeded its 5s limit in 1 of 3 runs, although that host process had already ended when the check finished. The observer checked the process only when a file event arrived in the configuration directory; the host removes `endpoint.json` before it exits and a process exit creates no file event, so when that event arrived before the process ended, the exit was never checked again. The missing completion signal was a process exit notification. The observer now waits for the exit with macOS `caffeinate -w`, which waits for the pid to end through the kqueue `NOTE_EXIT` notification, waits for the removal of `endpoint.json` through file events, and on failure reports both states with their measured times. The limit stays at 5s. Both rebuilt hosts passed 10 of 10 runs: Wails removed `endpoint.json` at 1–14ms and exited at 8–12ms; Tauri removed it at 30–47ms and exited at 30–39ms. Runs in which the removal time is later than the exit time are runs in which the file event arrived after the exit, which is why Tauri passed with the previous observer.

A separate manual-input observation prepared only the Tauri terminal, but the actual window status was `active:false`, `key:false`; no physical key was sent. Its empty 600-frame internal capture was deleted, and Tauri was closed through `host.quit`. The endpoint's `s`/`k` sequence therefore does not yet establish real IME composition or exactly-once PTY delivery. The exact cause is unknown; do not count the harness assertion or a direct `terminal.input` check as runtime IME evidence. Candidate pixels, cancellation/editing/range behavior in both hosts, and a real native IME exactly-once PTY result remain unproven. Completed on 2026-09-23: the false case is removed from the default window checks; the real input-method case is the activation-tier `e2e/activation/ime.test.mjs` under the F8-8 rule and passes on both rebuilt hosts with an exactly-once PTY result (F8-10); `make e2e-check` rejects `activate: true` outside `e2e/activation/`, and its injected test covers both locations. Candidate pixels and cancellation/range behavior remain under F8; the Wails shutdown timeout is F2-1.
  - [o] F8-4 — Correct the ambiguous native Ctrl-character failure path shown on 2026-09-23. Supported Ctrl inputs map from physical ANSI keys; unmapped native Ctrl keys now stop at the native region and report the physical key code and character scalar instead of forwarding a `Char` that can only fail later as `Unsupported`. Direct sidecar Ctrl errors also identify the rejected character and Unicode scalar. Red/Green: `image_region_test` TEST 13b verifies Korean `ㅕ` (`U+3155`) with physical key code 104 returns exactly one explicit diagnostic and no substitute key event; `unsupported_ctrl_character_error_identifies_the_received_scalar` verifies the sidecar error. Full `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, and `make docs-check` pass; both hosts were rebuilt. The screenshot's exact physical key and live UI result remain unverified, so only the generic native failure path is closed; actual host IME behavior remains open under F8/F8-3.
  - [o] F8-5 — Diagnose and fix Korean terminal input loss, missing preedit display, or stalls. Preserve text typed before IME use and every committed Hangul syllable, including `ddd` followed by `한글` yielding exactly `ddd한글`. During composition, visibly render successive preedit states such as `ㅎ → 하 → 한` at one caret position; do not write partial syllables to the PTY, and commit the final text exactly once in order. Provide a declared `terminal.compose.update` command for deterministic preedit-path verification, and observe its state through declared status and its pixels through native capture; separately verify the real OS IME callback path. Add a bounded, declared diagnostic trace of native `key`, `compose`, and `insert` callbacks and ordered PTY input so a failing real-IME run records evidence for locating loss; do not infer callback order from synthetic command tests. Keep this distinct from F8-3's synthetic `s`/`k` assertion and F6 selection errors. Compare the native input-client lifecycle with established terminal implementations; do not assume a web-terminal input model applies. Plugin Red reproduced loss for `compose("한") → insert("한") → insert("글")`; the implementation clears and queues preedit before committed input and passes that synthetic test, but this did not establish real macOS IME correctness. The rebuilt Tauri trace from the user's failing run recorded `insert("ㅎ")`, then `compose("ㅏ")`, `compose("ㄴ")`, `compose("ㄱ")`, `compose("그")`, `compose("글")`, `compose("글 ")`, and final `insert("글 ")`; the terminal handler explicitly discards compatibility-jamo inserts. This confirms the application discards input the native client reports, explaining at least the missing initial jamo; why macOS enters this callback sequence and how to preserve correct syllable composition remain unresolved. The earlier empty trace was also a diagnostic implementation defect: native `key` callbacks were not recorded. F8-5 remains incomplete. `terminal.compose.update` reports injected preedit state but is not evidence for the OS callback route. Verify callback trace, PTY bytes, composition pixels, exactly-once `ddd한글`, continued typing, and recovery on rebuilt Tauri and Wails. Both macOS host adapters call the shared `native/darwin` image-region implementation; change its input-client behavior in that owning platform package, run its native tests, and still validate each rebuilt host independently. Do not attribute this to F6 selection errors. Done on 2026-09-23 with the activation tier on the rebuilt Tauri and Wails hosts of `6e902c2` (started from `endpoint.json` after a 2 s wait because of G4-2): `e2e/activation/ime.test.mjs` starts `terminal.ime.trace`, types `ddd` with ABC and `한글`, Space, Enter, then `한`, Space, Enter with Korean 2-Set, and asserts through declared status, screen, and trace that the preedit shows ㅎ, 하, 한, ㄱ, 그, 글 in order, the block cursor covers both cells of the wide preedit and its right half is drawn, the native inserts and the terminal input queue are each exactly `ddd한글 한 ` in order, no jamo reaches the PTY, the shell runs `ddd한글` and then `한` once each, and Enter follows the committed text. The comparison with other input clients is the native activation test: an AppKit `NSTextView` receives the same keys correctly, and a document-less client of the kind terminals use loses the first syllable after a source switch (F8-10). Editing a composition with Backspace is F8-20.
    Failed initial shared-platform experiment: changing only the common image region's superclass to `NSTextView` while retaining the custom input-client state did not pass the endpoint key-injection probe. Tauri's compose status stayed empty for 20 seconds; Wails stayed at `ㅏ` for 20 seconds. That probe does not establish manual OS IME behavior. The incomplete attempt was reverted. The current implementation is a separate complete integration: `NSTextView` now owns marked-text state and standard key interpretation, while the terminal raster remains the visible renderer. It passes native synthetic state tests, but both rebuilt hosts and physical Korean IME remain unverified.
    The terminal plugin no longer suppresses compatibility-jamo inserts. Red: its previous Unicode-range filter produced zero PTY writes for native inserts `ㅎ` and `ㄴ`. Green: the filter is removed and the regression confirms both exact UTF-8 inputs reach the sidecar. This protects literal input from silent loss; the real IME must still deliver proper marked text through the shared native client before F8-5 can pass.
    Additional cancellation Red: `compose("한글") → compose("")` wrote the previous marked text to the PTY even though no `insert` callback committed it. Green removes that synthetic commit; only `insert` callbacks write committed text, while an empty `compose` clears/cancels preedit. The focused regression fails before and passes after the correction. This closes that plugin cancellation defect, not the outstanding native/host IME validation.
  - [o] F8-6 — Diagnose the reported slow window/divider drag with a measured Red reproduction before changing code. Identify whether the gesture moves the macOS application window or a soksak divider; record pointer-to-layout/presentation latency and complete capture on the current Tauri build, inspect the responsible event/render path, then add a regression and verify the correction without weakening drag or frame-completeness criteria. No cause or implementation change is established yet. Diagnosis on 2026-09-24: the user identified the gesture as a split-divider drag. A transcript of a 10-step divider drag shows one preparation (`syncSurfaces`), one drawn commit, and one `presentSurfaces` per step, 20 steps in about 400ms on both hosts, so the layout pipeline keeps pace with the pointer steps. Recordings (V5-12) show the DOM card and rail moving on every step while the browser document region, a separate WKWebView, trails the DOM by one to two steps while the layout moves and aligns again at rest. The native geometry is committed after the main document confirms presentation, and WebKit draws the DOM in its own process, so holding the window's layer transaction does not hold the DOM change; `-[NSWindow disableScreenUpdatesUntilFlush]` at the start of each preparation did not change the result. The alignment recording last passed at `ae687ce` (F1.1), before surfaces moved into the app DOM with document regions. Remaining: a design that shows DOM and native geometry in the same frame, then its regression and correction. Done on 2026-09-24: two causes were measured, and both came from `dcbca83` (F1.3-1). (1) The layout queue ran every waiting layout in arrival order. A presentation waits for the next app DOM presentation, which is at least one display frame, and pointer steps arrive every 16ms, so waiting layouts accumulated and the cards trailed the pointer by up to 100–140ms. The queue again keeps only the newest waiting layout, as step 5 of the placement sequence states; a replaced layout runs neither its preparation nor its draw, and the page writes the number of replaced layouts to the application log when the presentation settles. (2) The rail outline read the grid, which already holds the next prepared layout, while the cards show the layout of the last draw, so the rail led the cards by up to 25px. `SoksakView.painted(id)` now returns the rectangle the last draw wrote, and the rail uses it. After these corrections the worst drag delay was about 56ms on Wails and 62–94ms on Tauri: WebKit enables `PreferPageRenderingUpdatesNear60FPSEnabled` by default, so the app DOM updated its rendering near 60fps and each placement waited for that rendering. Registration of the app DOM webview now disables this WebKit feature and fails when the feature is missing; `host.window` reports `near60fps` per webview. `native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned` now also measures the delay: the host records each step time on the recording clock (`ticks`), the page reports the grid boundary after each step (`boundary`), and every recorded frame must show a layout at most 50ms after the grid left it. Red: the old builds failed on the rail at frame 17 (Wails) and 15 (Tauri); with the queue and rail corrections the delay failed at 50.8ms and 60.8ms; `window_facts_test` failed without the setting. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: 10 of 10 tracked repeats passed with a worst delay of 18.7–26.8ms in the measured runs; `three terminals survive repeated divider drags and project returns` and `shell divider drag does not leave a white surface frame` passed, so the replaced waiting layouts did not remove the round-trip end states. A 34ms limit fails the first drag after an application start (37.8–39.4ms); reaching it is registered as F8-6-2.
  - [o] F8-6-1 — P1: Make `host.window.presented` wait until the main page has drawn its current DOM. On 2026-09-23 `three terminals survive repeated divider drags and project returns` failed on both rebuilt hosts with 10 of 11 drag extremes: the drag ends at the start position and `core.grid` returns there, but the last recorded frames, including one 8ms after the reported `displayed` time, still show the high end. The settle step behind `host.window.presented` forces DOM layout with `evaluateJavaScript` and waits for the next display frame; it does not wait for the web content process to commit the rendering of that DOM, which is what the [exposure specification](spec/exposure.md) promises. Acceptance: the settle step waits for the web content presentation update after the DOM evaluation; the drag e2e case passes on both rebuilt hosts; the private API inventory records the call and its purpose. Done on 2026-09-23: `settle` (`native/darwin/src/surface_layout.m`) now waits after the DOM evaluation for the web content process to commit the rendering of that DOM through `_doAfterNextPresentationUpdate:`, and then reports the display time through the display link. On a round-trip failure the drag check now reports the frame count, first and last frame times, last presentation time, drag duration, and late steps; this measurement located the cause: before the change the recording ran 8ms past the last presentation time and still lacked the last two steps (539, 456). Red: before the change the case failed every time on both hosts. Green: on both rebuilt hosts the case and `multiple terminals keep fixed cells during a divider drag` passed 3 runs in a row each; `make -C native/darwin test`, `make native-test`, and `pnpm test` pass; the private API inventory records the call and its purpose. The full e2e run on the same day has four existing failing cases on both hosts that this change does not cause: they also fail on a build with the `settle` change reverted, and they are registered as V5-11 to V5-14.
  - [o] F8-6-2 — P2: Reduce the drag-delay limit of `native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned` from 50ms to 34ms (two 60Hz frames). On 2026-09-24 the first `native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned` run after a rebuild measured 39.4ms (Wails) and 38.3ms (Tauri) at steps 5–9, and a later first run after a restart measured 37.8ms on Tauri; other runs measured 18.7–33.3ms. Acceptance: identify what the first drag does that later drags do not (for example code that has not been compiled yet or a first raster), record the measured cause, set the limit to 34ms, and pass the first run after a restart and 10 tracked repeats on both rebuilt hosts. Progress on 2026-09-25, on the builds of V5-64 at load averages of 5–6: with the limit at 34 ms, the first run after a restart failed on both hosts once (38.8 ms and 40.8 ms) and passed on both after another restart (32.3 ms and 29.1 ms); ten later runs measured 16.0–29.1 ms except three spikes of 39.6, 51.2, and 71.6 ms outside the first run. The limit stays at 50 ms. Remaining: a per-frame trace of the late steps to find what the first drag and the spikes do. Progress on 2026-09-25, on the builds of V5-6-5 (Wails and Tauri, 120 Hz built-in display, load averages 3–8.6): `diagnostics.drag` now reports `layouts`, the begin, app DOM presentation, and commit times of each native layout transaction on the clock of the recorded frames (`surfaceLayoutTraceStart` and `surfaceLayoutTraceStop` in `surface_layout.m`, checked by `surface_layout_test`), and the check reports these stages for its slowest frame. With the limit at 34 ms, the first run after a restart passed on both hosts (20.7 ms and 26.0 ms), but the tracked repeat failed on Tauri in run 8 of 10 (62.7 ms), and 28 further runs measured 17.7–32.9 ms with seven runs at 34.1–64.2 ms. The trace gives the cause, and it is not specific to the first drag: the page starts the next preparation only after the previous transaction commits (docs/spec/native-surfaces.md), so a step that arrives just after a transaction began waits up to one transaction; the app DOM confirms each transaction's presentation after 13–17 ms (two 120 Hz refreshes), and after 20–25 ms (three refreshes) in the slow runs, whose preparations also began 4–9 ms after the previous commit instead of 1–2 ms; the commit then appears at the next refresh, up to 8.3 ms later. One transaction of waiting plus one of its own plus one refresh reaches about 38 ms without load, so the 34 ms limit cannot hold under the specified serialized presentation. The limit stays at 50 ms. Remaining: a user decision between keeping the specified 50 ms and changing the presentation pipeline so that a preparation does not wait for the previous commit. Done on 2026-09-25: the limit is 34 ms. On Wails and Tauri rebuilt at 77a42c4 with the 120 Hz built-in display and load averages 3.4–3.7, the first run after a restart measured 21.9 ms (Wails) and 31.9 ms (Tauri), and `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e run repeat --file outside.test.mjs --name "native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned" --count 10` passed 10 of 10 runs on both hosts; six further first runs after restarts at load averages 7–8.5 measured 24.9–29.2 ms. Measured cause of the first drag: its slowest frame is always at steps 4–5, where the app DOM confirms presentation 15–20 ms after a transaction begins instead of 13–15 ms later in the drag. At load averages of 8 and above, other processes delay that confirmation to 20–37 ms, and single frames exceed 34 ms (66.4 ms at load 8.0), so a failure of this check is reported with the load average and the transaction stages. Starting a preparation while a presentation waits, so that it replaces the waiting one, was measured and rejected: the replaced layout never reached the screen, and ten runs measured 25.6–33.1 ms against 18.2–30.1 ms without it.
  - [o] F8-6-2-1 — P2: Keep the first drag after a restart within the 34 ms limit at the load of normal work. On 2026-09-25, with the check windows uncovered and load averages of 5.9–7.0, `native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned` measured 37.4 ms at step 5 of the first drag after a restart, where the app DOM confirms presentation 15–20 ms after a transaction begins instead of 13–15 ms later in the drag; F8-6-2 recorded that only load averages of 8 and above exceeded the limit. Acceptance: measure what the first drag's early transactions do that later ones do not, remove that cost without a warm-up gesture, and pass the first run after a restart and 10 tracked repeats on both hosts at load averages up to 7. Progress on 2026-09-25: `diagnostics.drag` reports `handled`, the time the page spends applying each step, and the alignment check prints it. With the check windows uncovered and Finder active, the page applied every step in 0–1 ms in the first and later drags; in a first run after a restart that measured 41.4 ms at step 8 (Wails), the transaction for that step began 1.2 ms after the step, the app DOM confirmed its presentation at 15.2 ms and the commit followed at 16.0 ms, but the recording showed the new layout only at 41.4 ms, so 25 ms passed between the commit and the display; later drags showed a commit within one or two refreshes. Remaining: find what delays the display of the first drag's commits after they leave the application. Measured on 2026-09-25 after restarts, with the windows uncovered and load averages of 4.5–5.5, by dragging the same range twice and then a range of sizes not seen before: Wails 31.4, 19.8, and 26.4 ms; Tauri 34.5, 14.3, and 20.7 ms. Sizes not seen before are slower than a repeated range but not as slow as the first drag. In the first drag's slowest frame the step arrived while the previous transaction was open and waited about 10 ms for its commit, its own transaction took 15 ms to the app DOM presentation, and its commit reached the display about 4 ms later, which is the phase of the serialized pipeline that F8-6-2 measured at about 38 ms without load. Remaining: a change of the presentation pipeline that removes the wait for the previous transaction without leaving any step's layout undisplayed; the replacement tried in F8-6-2 left replaced layouts undisplayed and was slower. Latest tracked repeats on 2026-09-27 reached Wails run 9/10 before one 34.1 ms frame at load averages 5.7/7.8/10.9, and Tauri failed run 1 at 109.4 ms at 8.6/8.3/11.1; the measured transactions show serialized presentation waits rather than a missing frame. A clean Wails rerun after disk cleanup passed the focused long-history check, but the drag limit remains open until the pipeline removes this wait under the stated load. Done on 2026-09-27: `onLayout` starts native preparation before the page draw and waits for the preparation only after DOM work is scheduled, removing the duplicate WebKit/native frame wait while preserving the transaction order. Red: before the change, tracked repeats measured Wails 34.1 ms and Tauri 109.4 ms under observed load. Green: rebuilt Wails and Tauri each passed 10/10 tracked repeats at the unchanged 34 ms limit; the focused long-history check also reported 929/929 lines.
  - [o] F8-6-3 — P1: Keep the drag delay within the 50ms limit in the full default run. On 2026-09-24 `native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned` measured 66.0ms on Wails and 64.1ms on Tauri in the full default run, while separate runs of the same builds measured 18.7–39.4ms. Acceptance: measure what differs in the full run (number of windows, surfaces, sessions, or load), correct the cause, and pass the check within the full default run on both hosts. Progress on 2026-09-24: the delay does not depend on the full run. With the load average at 14–17 on 11 processors from other processes (Go builds, `dsymutil`, `node`), separate runs of the check on the builds of 6ff096e measured 65.6–147.4ms on both hosts, and the builds of 333a2df, where separate runs had measured 18.7–39.4ms earlier that day, measured 61.2–135.5ms under the same load; the delay follows the load of the machine, not a change in the code. The failure message now reports the load average and the processor count. Remaining: pass the check within the full default run on both hosts while no other process loads the machine. Done on 2026-09-24: after V5-33, the full default run `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e verify` on the builds of a47904b, at a load average of 5.9–9.8, passed `native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned` on both hosts within the run; 136 of 139 checks passed. The three failures were Tauri terminal service checks that found 103–104 shells for 3–4 terminals, left by the two earlier broken runs of V5-33 (their ages matched those runs); with the check services restarted the three checks passed, and the leak is registered as V5-34. Standalone runs at a load average of 3.5–5.4 passed 3 of 3 on each host.
  - [o] F8-6-4 — P1: Decide how fast round-trip drag checks treat replaced layouts. On 2026-09-24 `shell divider drag does not leave a white surface frame` failed once on Tauri in the full default run with `expected 4 complete round trips, observed high,low,high,low,high,low,high`: since F8-6 the page runs only the newest waiting layout, so the start position, which the pointer holds for one 16ms step between round trips, was not presented in one trip. On 2026-09-24 `three terminals survive repeated divider drags and project returns` failed the same way in 1 of 8 tracked Tauri runs (`expected 5 complete round trips, observed low,high,low,high,low,high,low,high,low`; the first return reached 539 and turned back before 456 was shown). Acceptance: the specification states whether a layout held for less than one presentation must reach the screen; the check measures the recorded positions against that rule; the check passes on both hosts in the full default run. Done on 2026-09-24 with the recommended rule, since the user did not choose another ("건너뛰어도 됨" was proposed): a layout that is replaced before it is presented does not reach the screen, and the native surfaces specification now states that a position held for less than one presentation may be absent from a recording. `assertRoundTrips` requires the start position at the beginning and the end and the far end in every round trip. The fast drag checks (`shell divider drag does not leave a white surface frame`, `three terminals survive repeated divider drags and project returns`, `multiple terminals keep fixed cells during a divider drag`) use the new `assertHeldStatesShown`: from the page-reported boundary per step and the host step times, every layout held for 34ms or longer must appear in the recording after it starts. Red: the unit tests for the new rules failed before the helpers changed. Green: the unit tests pass; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the white frame check passed 6 tracked repeats, the three-terminal round-trip check 4, and the fixed-cell drag check 3 runs.
  - [o] F8-7 — P0: Commit marked text exactly once when AppKit calls `unmarkText`. The code-review hypothesis that `unmarkText` discards marked text was refuted by measurement: the inherited `-[NSTextView unmarkText]` reports an empty `compose` and then one `insert` with the marked text through the overridden callbacks, so the text is committed. The override in the shared Darwin image region then reported a second empty `compose` after the commit, and native TEST 8 checked only that text storage was empty. Red: the strengthened TEST 8 decodes the reported events and fails because a preedit event follows the commit. Green: the redundant `unmarkText` override is removed; TEST 8 passes and verifies exactly one `insert` with the marked text, no preedit event after it, and no event without marked text; a mutation that replaces the commit with a preedit clear fails three TEST 8 checks. A terminal plugin regression verifies that the reported sequence writes the text to the PTY once; a mutation that commits the cleared preedit fails it. The [terminal runtime](spec/terminal-runtime.md) states the rule. `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, and `make docs-check` pass and both hosts were rebuilt. `make native-test` failed twice in `webview_geometry_test` with 10-second WebKit waits; after the V5-2 correction the complete native gate passes. Completion also requires a rebuilt Tauri and Wails check that makes AppKit accept active marked text and proves one PTY commit through declared command, status, and screen output; that check depends on the F8-8 route. Done on 2026-09-24: the remaining real-application check is the tracked activation-tier window check `e2e/activation/marked-text.test.mjs`. In a user-approved run with the Japanese (Romaji) input method enabled, `n i h o n n` typed into the terminal of both rebuilt applications made the input method report the marked text `n`, `に`, `にh`, `にほ`, `にほn`, and `日本` (live conversion) while nothing reached the PTY. Switching to ABC ended the composition: the native client and the terminal input queue each reported one insert of `日本`, no preedit followed the commit, and the shell printed `日本: command not found` once. Both hosts pass, and `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e verify:activation`, including the Korean check, passes 4 of 4.
  - [o] F8-8 — P0: Define a valid verification route for OS input-method behavior. Measured on 2026-09-23 with the user's approval for activation runs, using the activation-tier `image_region_ime_test` (`make test-activation`). A window that is not the key window of the active application does not reach the input method (the F8-3 probe). `CGEventPostToPid` delivered no key. In an active key window, keys sent through the endpoint injector route (`-[NSWindow sendEvent:]`) reach the Korean input method: an AppKit `NSTextView` control in the same window produces exactly `ddd한글 ` from ABC `ddd`, Korean 2-Set `g k s r m f`, and Space. Running the test executable outside the tool shell sandbox gave the same results, and the logged `error messaging the mach port for IMKCFRunLoopWakeUpReliable` also appears in runs whose control passes, so neither explains the failure. No route reaches the input method without activation. Rule: input-method behavior is verified only by activation-tier checks run with the user's approval, and each run includes the AppKit text-view control; AGENTS.md, the [verification contract](spec/verification.md), and the [exposure specification](spec/exposure.md) state it. The same run reproduces the product defect in F8-10.
  - [o] F8-9 — P1: Establish whether the `NSTextView` superclass adopted in `a0c6aa8` is required. The change was chosen after the endpoint probe that F8-8 identifies as invalid. `NSTextView` also carries text-editing behavior (automatic substitution, spelling correction, text replacement, services) that the terminal input client does not declare. Acceptance: through the F8-8 route, compare a plain `NSView` input client and the `NSTextView` client on the same Korean sequences; keep the simpler client that passes; if `NSTextView` remains, tests prove each inherited editing behavior is disabled or has no effect on terminal input. Done on 2026-09-24: through the user-approved F8-8 route (an active key window, `image_region_ime_test`) three clients received the same Korean sequence (ABC `ddd`, 2-Set `g k s r m f`, Space). A minimal `NSView` client without a document committed `dddㅎㅏㄴ글 `: the input method edits text it inserted earlier through replacement ranges (`{3, 1}`), so a client needs an input document. A plain `NSView` client with its own document and the `NSTextView` control both produced `ddd한글 `. The simpler client is kept: the image region is now an `NSView <NSTextInputClient>` with its own input document (string, marked range, selected range) instead of an `NSTextView`, and `unmarkText` clears the marked text and then inserts it, reporting in the same order as before. Twenty lines of text view configuration and the inherited editing (automatic substitution, spelling, services, undo) are gone, so no proof that they are disabled is needed. During the change TEST 8 caught a defect: `NSIntersectionRange` turned an empty range at the end of the document into `{0, 0}`, which inserted appended text at the start; a function that clamps ranges into the document corrects it. TEST 8 now asserts that the input responder is a text input client that does not inherit `NSText`. `make -C native/darwin test` passes, `image_region_ime_test` passes all 103 checks, and on both rebuilt hosts `verify:activation` (Korean and Japanese marked text) passes 4 of 4. In the default window checks the earlier failing cases pass after the applications restart; the remaining failure is V5-12. Activation checks leave their terminals open and disturb the next default run; that is registered separately.
  - [o] F8-9-1 — P0: Correct the Tauri crash that the F8-9 input client change introduced. On 2026-09-24, with `bb6a86c`, alternating `verify:activation` and `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e run repeat --file projects.test.mjs --name tauriv2 --count 2` on Tauri failed within one to three cycles in four attempts: the host either stopped answering (the main thread waited on the overlay webview mutex that no thread held) or ended with `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` at address 0x10 in WebKit `RemoteLayerTreeDrawingAreaProxy::commitLayerTree`. With `image_region.m` from before `bb6a86c` six cycles passed, and with `NSZombieEnabled` six cycles passed with no zombie message, which indicates freed memory used outside Objective-C messages. The `NSTextView` client ended its input context when it left a window; the plain `NSView` client did not. Acceptance: the client ends its input context when it leaves a window, and the same cycles pass on both hosts. Done on 2026-09-24: the input client is an `NSTextView` again. Ending the input context when the plain `NSView` client left a window did not remove the crash (the same `overlay_hide` stall in cycle 9 of 10), and a check that the closed region's context was not left current passed without that change, so it could not tell the clients apart and was not kept. The `NSTextView` client from before `bb6a86c` passed all 10 of the same cycles (`verify:activation` followed by `projects.test.mjs` twice). Following the other acceptance path of F8-9, more inherited editing is turned off (text checking types, find bar, font panel, ruler, graphics, link tool tips) and the Services requestor is removed; TEST 8 asserts that each edit is off, that Services cannot read or replace the input document, that the view is never a pointer, menu, or drag target, and that quotes, dashes, `(c)`, and `teh` are committed unchanged. `make -C native/darwin test` passes and `image_region_ime_test` passes all 103 checks. On the rebuilt hosts Tauri passed 5 of 5 cycles; the Wails failure in cycle 5 was the check harness overwriting `projects.json` non-atomically so that the application read a partial file (registered as V5-14-1), with no crash or stall. The specification describes the text view client and the edits it turns off.
  - [o] F8-10 — P0: Compose the first Hangul syllable after an input-source switch correctly in the terminal. Red (activation-tier `image_region_ime_test`, 2026-09-23): typing ABC `ddd`, switching to Korean 2-Set, and typing `g k s r m f` and Space commits `dddㅎㅏㄴ글 ` in the image region instead of `ddd한글 `; the user's report `ddd한글` → `ddd글` is the same defect combined with the earlier plugin filter that discarded jamo inserts. Measured cause: with a client that holds a document, the Korean input method edits committed text through `insertText` with a replacement range (`insert(ㅎ)`, `insert(하, {3,1})`, `insert(한, {3,1})`, …) and never uses marked text; the image region clears its text storage after every insert, and a minimal client without a document receives the same broken sequence (`insert(ㅎ)`, `mark(ㅏ)`, `insert(ㅏ)`, …), so the input method cannot combine the first syllable. The terminal cannot retract bytes already written to the PTY. Acceptance: the specification defines how input-method replacements of previously inserted text reach the terminal without writing partial syllables; the activation-tier test produces exactly `ddd한글 ` in the image region with the AppKit control passing; plugin tests cover the replacement sequence; both rebuilt hosts pass an activation-tier check with declared exposure. Green in the shared Darwin input client: while an input method is selected, the image region keeps text the input method inserted in its document, commits the text before the start of the latest insert as one `insert`, reports the rest as preedit, and commits the remainder, ends the composition, and clears the document before a special key, command, focus loss, input-source change, or close; keyboard-layout text is committed at once; AppKit text-editing substitutions are disabled. A first attempt that removed committed text from the document broke the next syllable (`ddd한ㄱㅡㄹ`) because the input method remembers document positions, so committed text now stays until the composition ends. The activation-tier test passes with the control: the region reports preedit `ㅎ`, `하`, `한`, commit `한`, preedit `ㄱ`, `그`, `글`, commit `글`, commit ` `, then the Enter key. Synthetic TEST 8/14 now check committed text after an explicit commit point, independent of the selected input source; a plugin regression replays the measured sequence and fails under the former insert-drop mutation. `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make docs-check`, `make platforms`, and `make hosts-check` pass, and both hosts were rebuilt. Host evidence on 2026-09-23: both hosts expose the diagnostic `diagnostics.input.source`; the activation-tier window check `e2e/activation/ime.test.mjs` (`pnpm -F @soksak/e2e verify:activation`) activates the application, types ABC `ddd`, switches to Korean 2-Set, types `g k s r m f`, Space, and Enter through `input.key`, and observes the `terminal.compose` sequence `ㅎ`, `하`, `한`, `ㄱ`, `그`, `글`, ` ` and the screen rows `sh-3.2$ ddd한글` and `sh: ddd한글: command not found`. It passes on the rebuilt Tauri build and the rebuilt Wails build, each run alone. Host Red: a Wails build of the pre-fix image region (`3b0a5c9`) fails the same check at the first key (`terminal.compose` stays empty for 20 seconds); the fixed rebuild passes again. The user also typed `ddd한글` manually in the rebuilt application and confirmed the result; the preedit cursor width is tracked as F8-11.

On 2026-09-23 the user observed in the rebuilt application that the block cursor over a composing Hangul syllable covers one cell while the syllable occupies two cells. Acceptance: measure the cursor and preedit cell widths through declared status and native capture on both hosts; a wide preedit character is fully covered by the cursor; narrow preedit and the cursor outside composition keep their widths; Red and Green tests in the owning package. Red: the new `frame_test` case measured 0.00 bright coverage in the second cell of a wide preedit; Green: all 12 frame tests pass, including a wide committed character and a narrow preedit that keeps a one-cell cursor. `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make docs-check`, and `make terminal-protocols-check` pass, and both hosts were rebuilt. The first host measurement read cell (0,0) because `terminal.cursor` did not report the current cursor position (F8-12), so host evidence is pending. Host evidence on rebuilt Tauri and Wails (activation-tier `e2e/activation/ime.test.mjs`, window capture without focus, measured against the surface background): the cursor after ABC `ddd` covers its cell fully and the next cell not at all (1.0 / 0.0), and the cursor over the wide preedit `글` covers both cells (0.78 / 1.0, where the first value excludes the inverted glyph strokes). The crop was produced with an ad hoc PNG writer in the window check, which was removed; F11-3 provides a still capture for observation. The captures also showed a gap after each Hangul glyph, tracked as F8-13.
  - [o] F8-12 — P0: Report the current cursor position and focus in `terminal.cursor` and place the input-method caret there. The vt-core `screen` events carry the current cursor, but the terminal plugin updates `terminal.cursor` and `region.setCaret` only when the session is configured; during F8-11 the status reported row 0, column 0, and `focused: false` after typing into a focused terminal, so candidate windows are anchored to a stale caret. Red: a plugin test fed a `screen` event with cursor (7,3), focused, and observed `terminal.cursor` at (0,0) and unfocused. Green: every `screen` event updates position, visibility, and focus and moves `region.setCaret`; a cursor without integer `col`/`row` and boolean `visible`/`focused` is reported as an error and does not replace the status. F8-18 removed the bundled font, so on a Mac without an installed Hangul monospace family the gap returns and this item applies again. Done on 2026-09-24: the renderer measures the typographic advance of a wide cell's glyph and, when it is narrower than the two cells, draws the glyph at its own size shifted right by half the difference; wider glyphs keep their start at the first cell. The terminal runtime specification states the rule. The vt-core frame renderer hardcodes Menlo, which has no Hangul, so CoreText substitutes a proportional font; macOS includes no Korean monospace font, and none (for example D2Coding or Nanum Gothic Coding) is installed on this Mac. The user asked on 2026-09-23 for a configurable font with a monospace default. Green on 2026-09-23: `sidecars/vt-core/fonts/D2Coding.ttf` (D2Coding 1.3.3, with `D2Coding-OFL.txt`) is embedded in the service and loaded at service start, which fails when the font cannot be loaded; the font ships with the terminal sidecar, which renders the raster, rather than with the page package; `frame.m` renders with a font handle created from that data or from an installed family, and `frame_metrics` now measures the `M` glyph instead of passing its character code as a glyph number. Core settings gained a `string` type (`type`, `default`, `maxLength`) in plugin-api, the workbench, the settings UI, and the plugin specification; the terminal declares `font.family` with default `D2Coding`. The sidecar `font` operation resolves the bundled family or an installed family, rejects others with `{error, reason, operation: "font"}`, recalculates metrics, size, session, and raster, and answers `{event: "font", family}`; the page reports `terminal.session.font` from that answer and keeps a font error until a font is applied, including across `state`, `session`, and `theme` events that cleared it before. Red/Green: the default Hangul ink ended at 21 of 32px (1.31 cells) before and passes the 1.75-cell check after; frame, contract, plugin-api, workbench, and terminal plugin tests cover resolution, rejection, metrics, the string type, and error lifetime, with mutations failing the workbench string check and the plugin error check. Host evidence on rebuilt Tauri and Wails (`e2e/terminal-font.test.mjs`): `한` ink extends 1.85 cells with D2Coding, Menlo changes the cell width from 6.5 to 8, a missing family keeps Menlo and reports `not installed`, and restoring D2Coding clears the error. The first host runs exposed that the page treated the sent request as applied and that theme answers cleared the error, both corrected. The user's own Wails configuration runs a terminal service started at 06:17 from an earlier build, which keeps its previous renderer until that service restarts.

Acceptance: a frame test measures the ink of a wide preedit glyph in its second cell against the same committed glyph and fails before the correction; the continuation cell matches the engine spacer; both rebuilt hosts show the full glyph, measured from a capture. Green: continuation cells of a wide preedit have width 0 like the engine spacer; all 13 frame tests pass, and rebuilt Tauri and Wails measure `secondInk` 0.092 with the cursor still covering both cells. `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make docs-check`, and `make e2e-check` pass.

The `font` answer carries `family`, `system`, and `skipped`, and the page reports `font` and `fontSystem` in `terminal.session` with no font error; the font error handling added in F8-14 is removed and the earlier error clearing is restored (F8-17).   - [o] F8-16 — P0: Deliver the text that ends a Hangul composition without waiting for the next key. The user reported on 2026-09-23 that after composing `한글` a Space is needed to leave the composition, but the space does not appear. Under F8-10 the image region keeps the last input-method insert (here the space) as editable preedit until a later insert, command key, focus change, or input-source change, and the cursor is drawn at the start of the preedit, so the space is neither written to the PTY nor visible. Acceptance: measure on a rebuilt host what the PTY and the screen hold after `한글` and Space with no further key (Red); correct the rule so text that the input method no longer edits is committed without a later key and without writing partial syllables; the activation-tier check verifies the space on the screen before any further key on both hosts. Measured on 2026-09-23 with the activation tier on macOS 26.6.2: with an `NSTextView` client the Korean input method never uses marked text and inserts each jamo and replacement inside `-[NSTextInputContext handleTSMEvent:completionHandler:]`, while Space reaches the client from `-[NSKeyBindingManager flushTextForClient:]`; the public API gives both paths the same call, value class, and `handleEvent:` result. Red: `image_region_ime_test` reported preedit `' '` and committed `ddd한글` after Space for both `ddd한글` and a first syllable after a switch. Green: `SPInputMethodContext` (a subclass of the region's input context) marks the time inside that method, and the image region commits text that arrives outside it together with the text before it; the override is listed in the private native API inventory, and region creation fails when the selector is missing. `image_region_test` TEST 8 covers the rule, `make native-test` passes, and `image_region_ime_test` passes with preedit `''` and committed `ddd한글 ` and `한 ` before any further key and no error event. The rebuilt Tauri and Wails hosts pass `e2e/activation/ime.test.mjs`: after Space the compose status is empty and `terminal.cursor` moves from column 13 to 16 (글 and the space echoed by the shell) before Enter. The first Tauri run failed before any input with `window main does not exist` (G4-2); the rerun passed. The first `pnpm test` run failed in a vt-core test that depends on test order (V5-7); the rerun passed with `make docs-check`, `boundaries`, `exposure-check`, `parity-check`, `e2e-check`, `platforms`, and `hosts-check`.

Done on 2026-09-24: on rebuilt Wails and Tauri, `a newly split terminal presents its first native raster` took 400 ms on both hosts right after the check services were stopped and restarted, and 323.6 ms (Wails) and 340.2 ms (Tauri) with the running service, so a new service process adds at most about 80 ms; `verify-vt-recovery.mjs` measured the endpoint, which the service publishes after it loads the default font, 23–46 ms after start in eight runs. Acceptance: measure with the activation tier what the AppKit control and the image region produce for `gks` Backspace (Red); the specification states the editing rule; the region lets the input method edit its composition and the PTY receives only the final syllable; both rebuilt hosts pass the activation check.   - [o] F8-21 — P1: Stop reporting a surface error when a terminal selection covers no text. On 2026-09-24 the user's terminal card showed `invalidParams: selection is empty` in its status line. The terminal module sends `selection.start` when the pointer moves during a press; a drag over blank cells or a move inside one blank cell selects no text, and the sidecar then answers `selection.end` with that error, as the [terminal runtime](spec/terminal-runtime.md) specification currently requires. Such a drag is a normal user gesture. Acceptance: the specification states that a release whose selection covers no text clears the selection, leaves the clipboard unchanged, and reports `selection.end` with `copied: false` instead of an error; engine and serve-contract tests fail before the change; the terminal module accepts the event without a status error; a window check drags over blank cells on both rebuilt hosts and finds no surface error. Done on 2026-09-24: the terminal runtime specification now states that a release whose selection covers no text clears the selection, leaves the clipboard unchanged, and emits `selection.end` with `copied: false`. `Engine::selection_end` returns `Option<String>`; the Alacritty engine clears a selection without text and returns `None`, and the service answers `selection.end` instead of `invalidParams: selection is empty`. The terminal module accepts the event, and `terminal.session` reports `selecting` and `selectionReleases`. Red: `blank_selection_release_is_not_an_error` failed, the plugin test recorded `selection.end` as unsupported, and the new window check `a native drag over blank terminal cells ends without a surface error` reproduced the user's error on both hosts with the dragged blank cells left inverted. Green: the engine, serve-contract, and plugin tests pass; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the window check and `native terminal selection renders and copies through one explicit paste` pass. The window check waits for `selectionReleases`; an intermittent Wails failure in which the page does not receive the injected release is registered as F8-21-1.
  - [o] F8-21-1 — P1: Deliver every injected release of a terminal drag to the terminal page on Wails. On 2026-09-24 `a native drag over blank terminal cells ends without a surface error` failed on Wails in run 8 of 15, run 10 of 20, run 8 of 40, and run 39 of 40 of the tracked repeat, and passed 70 runs in a row with the call transcript enabled. In each failure the injected release completed after the page reported `click`, but `terminal.session` kept `selecting: true` and `selectionReleases: 0`, so the terminal view's `pointerup` handler did not run. Tauri did not fail. Acceptance: measure where the release is lost (native `mouseUp` target, WebKit pointer event, pointer capture) without the transcript, correct the cause, and pass 100 tracked repeats on both rebuilt hosts. Done on 2026-09-24: a temporary page-side record of pointer events showed `pointerdown`, `gotpointercapture`, `pointermove`, and then `click` with `buttons: 1` and no `pointerup` in a failed run. WebKit reads the pressed buttons of a mouse event from `+[NSEvent pressedMouseButtons]`, the system state, so a synthetic release while `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` is nonzero is a chorded move; the state report does not identify its source. A temporary record of that state at each injected release matched both measured failures (value 1) and about 470 passing releases (value 0). The host reported success because the release waited for `click`, although the exposure specification requires `pointerup`. `sp_input_pointer` now returns `SP_INPUT_BUTTON_HELD` for a press or release while `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` is nonzero, both hosts return 1007, and a release completes after the document receives `pointerup` and WebKit has processed pending mouse events, which includes the click. The specification lists 1007. Results on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: `a native drag over blank terminal cells ends without a surface error` passed 100 tracked repeats; the shell window checks passed 30 tracked repeats and 10 full-file runs, after one full-file failure of `shell commands run, report the directory, interrupt, and clear` on Wails whose message was not captured; the native suite passes. A nonzero AppKit button state during a run was not reproduced on purpose, so the 1007 path is verified by the measured cause and code review.

Done on 2026-09-24: the sidecar answers `selection.start`, `selection.update`, and `paste` with `{ack: true, event}`, and the terminal module recorded each as an unsupported event. Red: the plugin test listed all three in `unsupported`, and `a native drag over blank terminal cells ends without a surface error`, which now requires an empty `unsupported`, failed on both hosts. Green: the plugin tests pass, the window check passed 10 tracked repeats on rebuilt Wails and Tauri, and the selection and paste window checks pass.

  - [o] F8-17 — P1: Clear terminal session errors only through the operation that resolves them. A `state` event cleared every `terminal.session.error` except native image errors while the card kept showing the surface error, so a rejected font error disappeared from status during F8-14; F8-18 removed font errors and restored the earlier clearing, so the general rule remains open here. Acceptance: list each error source and the event that resolves it; a Red test per source shows an unrelated event hiding it; status and the surface indicator stay consistent. Done on 2026-09-23: [session errors](spec/terminal-runtime.md#session-errors) lists each error source and the event that resolves it. A snapshot source (`state`, `session`, `theme`, `screen`, `trace`) is resolved by the next valid event of the same kind; `sidecar`, `native image`, `input`, `key`, and `cursor` stay until the surface closes, because no later event shows that the failed operation succeeded, and a sidecar error reply does not name the rejected operation. The terminal keeps the latest error of each source and sets `session.error` to the most recent remaining one; the earlier blanket clearing (`state` cleared everything except native image errors, `session` and `theme` cleared everything) is removed. The two sources that raise the card indicator (`sidecar`, `native image`) are never resolved, so status and indicator show the error together. Red: all 10 new unit tests failed on the previous code: for the 5 persistent sources an unrelated `state` or `session` event removed the error, for the 4 snapshot sources an event of another kind removed it, and the case that shows the remaining error after a resolution failed. The existing test `a sidecar rejection is reported without DOM-driven retry` asserted that a valid `state` clears a rejection; it now asserts that the rejection stays. A new trace resolves the trace overflow, and the same test checks that the overflow stays after a `state` event. On both rebuilt debug applications the new e2e case `a terminal session error stays until the event that resolves it` passes: after the trace capacity is exceeded, a theme switch keeps the error and starting a new trace removes it. The terminal and font e2e files pass 44 of 46; the two failures are the drag round-trip failure recorded in F8-6.

Invalid folder operations return explicit `-32000` errors and the library remains available. Forced SIGINT fixture teardown socket warnings are recorded as shutdown noise, not a test result.
