Red: the implementation and test existed, but `node scripts/check-test-parity.mjs` rejected completed F9 because no linked feature entry named them. Green adds the `e2e/library.test.mjs` implementation/test link, preserves F9 as complete, and passes the parity and documentation audits.
- [o] F10 — Restore both hosts' existing native modal contract, without redesigning the UI. F10.1–F10.4 cover the rebuilt Tauri and Wails child-WebView lifecycle, transparent split menus, semi-transparent settings scrim, focus and dismissal, stale ordering, reload cleanup, movement, resize, and explicit geometry-verification pending state.
  - [o] F10.1 — The current macOS Tauri build passes all seven modal cases: transparent picker menus, semi-transparent settings scrim, native focus, stale-answer ordering, reload cleanup, parent resize, and modal coverage. The native picker keeps Escape focus in its child WebView.
  - [o] F10.2 — Wails now creates the same dedicated child WebView contract as Tauri: hidden transparent creation, `/overlay.html` loading, aligned placement, direct child-bridge content/position events, ready-time reveal and focus, native hit identity, focus return, and close/reload cleanup. The previous Red was `host.window.modal === null`; the rebuilt macOS Wails build passes all seven modal cases in 3.28s, including the held first-answer ordering, transparent picker menus, semi-transparent settings scrim, input blocking, resize, and stale cleanup.
  - [o] F10.3 — Transparent split picker and settings with a 50% black background plus 3px blur; retain the dedicated child-WebView contract and correct the conflicting main-DOM-only statement. Red: after closing one native picker, its deferred Tauri main-WebView focus restoration could run after the next picker opened and steal the replacement child WebView's Escape focus; the following split picker then remained open and later endpoint checks timed out. Green: Tauri `modal.test.mjs` 7/7 in 3.42s and Wails 7/7 in 3.04s on separately rebuilt fresh processes and configuration directories; the test covers modal content/order, stale answer ordering, layout replacement, reload cleanup, input blocking, transparent add/split menus, settings scrim/restoration, movement, and resize. Each modal test has a 30s test timeout. Tauri now skips deferred main-WebView focus restoration when a replacement modal is already open.
  - [o] F10.4 — Wails create/hide/load/place/ready/show/focus/update/close lifecycle and event routing. Both hosts: actual pixels, background input blocking, dismissal rules, focus return, move/resize/reopen/reload, and stale-response rejection. Red: a sequential rebuilt Wails modal run passed its seven assertions but emitted transient `V7a`, `V7c`, `V10`, and `R` failures while a new DOM layout was waiting for the native placement answer; this was a verification race, not evidence to discard. Green: `placementPending()` now identifies the unseated DOM commit and the verifier reports explicit `host placement pending` instead of a false geometry failure until the native answer arrives. The rebuilt Wails modal suite passes 7/7 in 2.86s with no verification failure; the focused compositor/workbench tests pass, and the test uses 30s per-case timeouts.
- [o] F11 — Complete exposure and diagnostic parity. The canonical exposure declarations, runtime binder audit, bounded capture contract, and language-lane supervisor now have current executable evidence. Red/Green coverage is recorded in F11-1 and F11-2; the current `pnpm test` workspace run, `make boundaries`, `make exposure-check`, `make docs-check`, `make language-test`, and `node scripts/check-test-parity.mjs` pass without hidden skips or fallback results.
  - [o] F11-1 — Declared commands/status/DOM entries reach actual behavior through the binder audit. Red was the ShadowRoot traversal defect that reported delegated shell controls as unbound. Green: the rebuilt Wails and Tauri `e2e/audit.test.mjs` each pass 1/1 across workspace controls, card menus/tab lists, rename states, settings scopes/sections, library forms/search, plugin surface documents, and the return path; no `unbound` entries are reported. The Wails close emitted two post-test writes to an already closed endpoint during cleanup, which is separate from the passing audit result and remains observable.
  - [o] Bounded capture returns actual count, limited, and longestGap on both hosts. Red was the Wails response omitting `limited`; the platform contract now reads the shared macOS cap state and returns it. Reaching the cap is normal; missing gesture coverage fails only measurement. Release excludes diagnostics; recordings are cleaned up. Wails payload tests pass for both capped and uncapped results.
  - [o] F11-2 — P1: Remove diagnostic terminal commands from release builds. `plugins/terminal/plugin.json` declares the `terminal.ime.trace` command and status and the `terminal.compose.update` injection command unconditionally, while AGENTS.md requires diagnostic code to exist only in diagnostic builds. Acceptance: classify each entry as diagnostic or user operation with its reason; diagnostic entries are absent from release staging and a mechanical check fails when a release manifest declares one; specifications and translations describe the result. Done on 2026-09-23. Classification: `terminal.compose.update` injects the preedit that the OS input method produces, so it is diagnostic; the `terminal.ime.trace` command and status record internal input events for checks, so they are diagnostic; the `terminal.compose` status reports the visible composition state and remains a user entry. The [plugin specification](spec/plugins.md#diagnostic-declarations) now defines a `diagnostics.json` file (`module`, `exposes`) at the plugin package root. That file and its module are absent from the package `files`, only `--diagnostics` staging copies them, and staging fails when either is listed. Staging always writes `/diagnostic-plugins.json`, which is `{}` in a release build. In a diagnostic build the workbench adds those declarations to the plugin surface declarations, imports the module, and passes it as the surface context `diagnostics`; in a release build it is `null`. The two terminal commands and the trace status moved to `plugins/terminal/diagnostics.json` and `ui/terminal-diagnostics.js`; `terminal.js` keeps only an input observer registration and `updateCompose`. `make release-check` runs the new `auditPluginDiagnostics` and fails when `/diagnostic-plugins.json` is not `{}`, a plugin diagnostic module is staged, or a staged file contains a diagnostic name. Red: before the declarations moved, `make release-check` on the real release staging failed and reported `terminal.ime.trace` and `terminal.compose.update` in `plugin.json` and `terminal.js`; the script test injects each failure; the staging tests fail when the module is copied without the flag or when the `files` check is removed. After the change `make release-check` passes; on both release applications the declaration list has no diagnostic name and running `terminal.ime.trace` returns `unknown command (1001)`. On both debug applications the new e2e case `diagnostic terminal entries inject preedit and record terminal input` passes, and the full terminal e2e file passes 40 of 42; the two failures are the drag round-trip failure recorded in F8-6. A silent `catch {}` found during the work is registered as F11-4.
  - [o] F11-3 — P1: Provide a still window capture for observation. `diagnostics.capture.start/stop` records raw BGRA frames for measurement, but no declared tool returns a viewable image, so during F8-11 an ad hoc PNG writer was added to a window check and then removed. Acceptance: the shared native capture produces a still image of a window without focusing it; both hosts expose it in diagnostic builds; the CLI can request it; the endpoint specification and operations document describe it; captures stay observation material and are removed after use; tests cover the native encoder and both host methods. Green on 2026-09-23: `sp_capture_still` in `native/darwin/src/capture.m` takes one ScreenCaptureKit screenshot of a current-process window without activating the application and writes a PNG with ImageIO; a window without an on-screen frame is an explicit error. `capture_test` covers a missing window, the device-pixel size (400x200 for a 200x100pt window at 2x, five consecutive runs), the window content, and no activation; a first version of the test measured 392-400 pixels because it captured during the window's appearance animation, which the test now disables. Both hosts expose `diagnostics.capture.still` in diagnostic builds, writing `window.png` in a private `captures/still-*` directory like recordings, and `soksak capture` requests it. On the rebuilt Tauri and Wails applications the command returned the library window image (2400x1520 and 2398x1518 pixels for the same 1200x760pt `host.window` frame; the one-point difference is the ScreenCaptureKit window rectangle), which was viewed and removed. The ad hoc PNG writer from F8-11 is not used.
  - [o] F11-4 — P1: Report malformed staged sidecar declarations in the release check. `scripts/check-release.mjs` reads each staged `sidecar.json` in `extractExecutableBasenames` inside `try { … } catch {}`, so a file that is not valid JSON yields no executable names and its executable is never scanned for diagnostic code, while the comment says another check handles it. Acceptance: a malformed staged `sidecar.json` fails `make release-check` with the file and parse error; a Red test in `scripts/test/soksak-scripts.test.mjs` injects one. Done on 2026-09-23: `executableBasenames` (formerly `extractExecutableBasenames`) throws with the file path and the error when a `sidecar.json` cannot be read as JSON or an executable path is not a string; the check reports that message as an error and continues with the next file. Red: the previous function returned an empty list for a malformed file, so the new test `release check reports a malformed staged sidecar declaration` failed with `Missing expected exception`. After the change the test and `make release-check` pass.
- [o] F12 — P1: Provide font-size zoom commands for cards and for the frame. The user asked on 2026-09-23 for commands bound to Command `+`, `-`, and `0` that enlarge, reduce, and restore the text size. In a card they apply to that card only and work for both DOM cards and native cards (for example terminal image regions); in the frame they apply to the whole window, including the frame. Acceptance: declared commands with status for each card's and the frame's size; shortcuts run the commands; the specification defines scope, limits, and persistence; tests cover a DOM card, a native terminal card, and the frame, and both rebuilt hosts show the changed sizes through status and numeric capture checks. Done on 2026-09-24 through F12-1, F12-2, and F12-3 ([text size](spec/text-size.md)): declared commands and the `core.text` status, View menu shortcuts in both hosts, scope by the last press, card factors saved with the layout and the frame factor as the common setting `textSize`; window checks on both rebuilt hosts cover a DOM card and the frame (element sizes), a native terminal card (cell size and glyph pixels), and a browser document (block pixels).
  - [o] F12-1 — P1: Implement the text size scope, commands, and status, the DOM card and frame rendering, persistence, and the View menu shortcuts of [text size](spec/text-size.md). Acceptance: `core.text.larger`, `core.text.smaller`, and `core.text.reset` are declared and change the scope's factor through the steps; `core.text` reports the scope and factors; a card press selects the card and a frame press selects the frame; card factors survive a reload and frame factors are the common setting `textSize`; both hosts' View menus run the commands with Command `=`, `-`, and `0`; unit tests and window checks on both rebuilt hosts measure a DOM card's text and the frame chrome growing in pixels while the plane stays inside the window. Done on 2026-09-24: `text-size.js` holds the steps and the scope; a card press selects the card and a press on the chrome bar, space bar, or project tabs selects the frame. `core.text.larger`, `core.text.smaller`, and `core.text.reset` change the scope's factor, `core.text` reports the scope and factors, card factors are stored through `Soksak.setData` in the card's layout data, and the frame factor is the common setting `textSize`. CSS `zoom` applies the frame factor to the chrome rows, and the product of the frame and card factors to each card's surface module host and sidebar set; the first row keeps at least the window title bar height. Both hosts' View menus have 글자 크게, 글자 작게, and 글자 기본 크기 with Command `=`, `-`, and `0`; each item sends `menu-command` to the application's main window, or its frontmost visible window when the application has never been active. Both hosts expose `host.menu.select` to perform a menu item. Red: the unit test failed without `text-size.js`, the host menu test and the `host.menu` window check failed without the items, and the first window runs found that the host found no main window in an application that was never active, that the layout library's card `data` is read-only, and in a capture that dividing the zoomed host's percentage size shrank the shell content to about 60% of its card. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: unit and host tests pass; `text size commands enlarge the pressed card or the frame and keep the plane in the window` passed 3 tracked repeats (the card factor survives a reload; the shell input and the space tab grow within 5% of the factor, since line heights round to pixels; the plane stays inside the window); captures with frame 1.5 and card 1.25 show the enlarged chrome and card text with the content filling its card on both hosts. Terminal and browser document content follow in F12-2 and F12-3.
  - [o] F12-2 — P1: Apply the effective text size to terminal cards. Acceptance: the terminal module sends the font size 13 × effective factor to the sidecar; the sidecar recomputes cell size, columns, and rows; sidecar and plugin tests fail before the change; a window check on both rebuilt hosts measures the terminal cell size and the rendered glyph height growing with the factor. Done on 2026-09-24: the sidecar `font` operation now requires `size` in points (4 to 128), keeps it for later raster configurations, recomputes the cell size, columns, and rows from it, and answers it in the `font` acknowledgement; the sidecar specification states it. The page gives each surface module `runtime.textSize` (`read` and `on`) with the frame factor times the factor of the card that holds the tab, and notifies it after every render and settings change; a tab that left the layout keeps its last factor until it is disposed. The terminal module sends the font family with 13 points times that factor and reports the applied size as `fontSize` in `terminal.session`. Red: `test_font_size_sets_the_cell_size` found the cell height unchanged by the size, and the plugin tests found no size in the font request. Green: the sidecar, plugin, workbench, and plugin API tests pass; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri `the terminal font follows the pressed card's text size` passes: after two steps the font is 16.25 points, the cell height grows within 0.1 of 1.25 times, and the red glyph height in the captured pixels within 0.15 of 1.25 times; `terminal-font.test.mjs` passes. In the same terminal run `three terminals survive repeated divider drags and project returns` failed once on Tauri, recorded under F8-6-4, and the keyboard check failed as V5-23 records.
  - [o] F12-3 — P1: Apply the effective text size to browser document regions. Acceptance: a host document operation sets and keeps the document page zoom across placement and scale changes; native, host contract, and plugin tests fail before the change; a window check on both rebuilt hosts measures the document's rendered content growing with the factor while the region stays on its element. Done on 2026-09-24: `sp_document_zoom` sets a document's page zoom and keeps it through placement and surface scale changes, rejecting a non-finite or non-positive zoom; both hosts add the document operation `documentZoom` (`DocumentZoom`, `document_zoom`), whose request zoom must be a finite positive number (contract case `documents.request.zoom-must-be-finite-positive`); the runtimes, the page's document port, and the plugin API region expose `zoom`; the browser module sets the zoom to its surface's text size factor at mount and on each change. The acceptance wording changed from "the region keeps its rectangle" to "the region stays on its element": the card factor also enlarges the address bar, so the document element becomes shorter. Red: `document_view_test` did not link without `sp_document_zoom`, and the browser plugin test found no zoom at mount. Green: the native, host contract (210 cases), plugin, and runtime tests pass; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri `the browser document zoom follows the pressed card's text size` measures a CSS 120×60 red block growing within 0.05 of 1.25 times in captured pixels after two steps while the region is placed on its element, and `browser.test.mjs` passes 10 of 10.
  - [o] F12-4 — P1: Keep divider drags presenting every frame after F12. On 2026-09-24 `multiple terminals keep fixed cells during a divider drag` failed in 5 of 5 Tauri runs with `divider recording contained too few frames: 14`–`16` after F12-1, and passed 3 of 3 with the CSS `zoom` declaration on the surface module hosts removed, although every factor was 1. Acceptance: no `zoom` is declared while the effective factor is 1; the check passes repeatedly on both rebuilt hosts; the text size window checks still pass. Done on 2026-09-24: the plane writes `data-text-zoom` and `--text-zoom` on a card only when its effective factor (frame × card) is not 1, and the settings write `data-frame-text` on the document root only when the frame factor is not 1; the CSS `zoom` rules apply only with these attributes, and the text size specification's rendering is unchanged. Red: the five failing Tauri runs above; the experiment without the declaration passed 3 of 3. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: the fixed-cell drag check passed 3 of 3 runs, and `text-size.test.mjs`, `the terminal font follows the pressed card's text size`, and `the browser document zoom follows the pressed card's text size` pass. The rebuild required freeing disk space: the data volume had 138MB left, and a leftover 681MB check recording and the Rust debug builds of this repository were removed.
- [o] F13 — P1: Make plugin sidebars show their sections and move sidebar and plugin configuration into a plugin-oriented settings window. Done on 2026-09-26: F13-1 through F13-4 pass on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts. All nine browser, files, and shell sections show declared plugin state; the manifest-based settings pages and sidebar set operations pass; and the browser empty state passes. The compatibility window checks after F13-3 and F13-4 also pass. Detailed evidence remains under F13-1 through F13-4.
  - [o] F13-0 — P2: Set the inset sidebar to its minimum width on a real double click of its grip and open a sidebar without a stored width at the minimum. Acceptance: validation rejects a section without a module and a set without a valid layout; in both apps a combined set shows every section in `list` layout, and `tabs` layout switches sections through a declared command with the selection kept per sidebar.   - [o] F13-1-1 — P2: Keep each sidebar's section selection and folded sections across a reload and a restart. F13-1 keeps them in page memory only. Acceptance: after selecting a tab and folding a section in a sidebar, reloading the page and restarting the application show the same selection and folds in both apps. Done: each sidebar's selected tab and folded sections are saved in the space layout as `sidebars` ([projects](spec/projects.md#persistence)), per space because a sidebar is identified by a card of that space. The `sidebar-sections.js` part landed in d5e71587 with the sidebar agent's commit; `plane.js` and `index.html` save and restore it. Red: `test/sidebar-choices.test.mjs` failed on the `sidebar-sections.js` before d5e71587, and `e2e/sidebar-choices.test.mjs` failed on both apps before the rebuild (the saved layout had no `sidebars`). Green: workbench unit tests pass, and on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the window check passes: the saved `projects.json` holds the tab and the fold, and the reloaded document shows them. A restart of both check applications followed by opening the project showed the folded 북마크 section again; the restart was done by hand, because window checks do not start applications.
  - [o] F13-2 — P1: Draw real content in the nine sections (browser: DOM, network, tabs, history; files: file tree, bookmarks; shell: run history, cwd, jobs). Acceptance: each section shows its plugin's current state through declared statuses in both apps. Done for the shell: a section follows its plugin's statuses through `context.status` and binds controls to its plugin's commands through `context.bind` (dom name `core.sidebar.section.control`); `core.sidebars` reports each section's text and control count. The shell sections show `shell.cwd`, `shell.history` (a click writes the line again through `shell.write`), and `shell.jobs` (a 중단 control runs `shell.interrupt`). Red: the workbench and shell unit tests failed before the change (`observe`, `context.bind`, `shell.history`, the section modules). Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts: `e2e/sidebar.test.mjs` `the shell sections show the shell's directory, written lines, and pending runs` and the combined-set check pass 4/4, and with `e2e/audit.test.mjs` and the inset check the run passes 8/8. Earlier progress had F13-2-1 and F13-2-2 open; both and the remaining sections were completed on 2026-09-26. All nine sections show plugin state in both apps through F13-2-1 to F13-2-5.
  - [o] F13-2-1 — P1: Draw the browser 탭 and 히스토리 sections. The browser plugin has no status for its session history and no command to go to an entry, and `plugins/browser/ui/browser.js` is being changed by another task. Acceptance: in both apps, after a browser tab loads two local pages, 히스토리 lists both entries from a declared browser status and a native click on the first entry loads it (`browser.location` reports its address); 탭 lists the browser tabs of the card with their titles from declared statuses and a native click selects a tab. Done: document-region state reports `history: {entries, index}` from the WKWebView back-forward list, and `go` accepts `entry` with a non-zero `offset` (native `sp_document_go(document, 4, offset)`, host contract `documents.request.entry-requires-offset` in both hosts, `region.entry(offset)` in the plugin API). The browser declares `browser.history` and `browser.history.go {index}`; sections may follow core statuses and bind core commands ([sections](spec/plugins.md#sections)); 히스토리 binds each entry to `browser.history.go` and 탭 follows `core.grid` and binds each browser tab of the card that holds its surface to `core.tab.select`. Red: `document_view_test` did not build against the old `sp_document_go`, the workbench core-status section case, the browser history and section cases, and both host contract tests failed before the change; the first window run of `the browser sections list the session history and the card's browser tabs` failed in both apps because the composition region handle had no `entry`, which the plugin API then gained with a unit case. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts: that check passes in both apps (history `onetwo`, a native click on the first entry loads it, the tabs list the card's two browser tabs, and a native click selects the first), `e2e/sidebar.test.mjs`, `browser.test.mjs`, `audit.test.mjs`, and `modal.test.mjs` pass 46/46, `make native-test` passes, the Wails and Tauri host tests and `make host-contract-check` (221 cases) pass, and package tests pass (browser 18, plugin-api 91, workbench 111).
  - [o] F13-2-2 — P1: Draw the browser DOM and 네트워크 sections. The WKWebView document region has no interface that reports its DOM tree or its network requests. Acceptance: shared native code used by both hosts reports the document's element tree and the requests of the loaded page as declared browser statuses (spec first in `docs/spec/native-surfaces.md`), and in both apps the sections show the elements and requests of a local test page. Done: a script in the host's content world (shared `document_view.m`, used by both hosts) posts the main-frame document's elements (`{depth, tag, id, class}`, at most 500) on parse and on each tree, `id`, or `class` change, and the requests its Performance API records (navigation, then resources, `{url, type, start, duration}`, at most 200) through a script message handler of that world; the region state carries them as `elements` and `requests`, cleared when a navigation starts, and the hosts forward the state unchanged ([document regions](spec/native-surfaces.md#document-regions), which states the Performance API limits: no methods, headers, bodies, status, or subframe requests). The browser reports `browser.elements` and `browser.requests`; the DOM section lists `tag#id.class` indented by depth and the 네트워크 section lists type, address, and duration, each stating truncation. `make exposure-check` now accepts core names in section modules, which F13-2-1 needed and had not passed. Red: `document_view_test` failed `the inspect document did not report its elements and requests`, and the browser section and status cases failed before the change. Green: `make native-test` passes; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts `the browser DOM and network sections list the document's elements and requests` passes in both apps (elements `html head title body div img`, requests navigation then `img /pixel`, and the section texts); `e2e/sidebar.test.mjs`, `browser.test.mjs`, `audit.test.mjs`, and `modal.test.mjs` passed 49 of 50 with the Wails shell sections case timing out once and then passing 5/5 in `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e run repeat`; package tests pass (browser 20, plugin-api 91, workbench 111).
  - [o] F13-2-3 — P1: Draw the files 파일 트리 and 북마크 sections. The files plugin has no surface, so `context.status` has no registrant, and no component lists a directory. Acceptance: the specification states where a plugin without a surface gets the project folder (`docs/spec/projects.md`) and its listing (a sidecar), and in both apps 파일 트리 lists the entries of the project folder of a disposable project and 북마크 lists stored bookmarks, each updated after a change. Done: a plugin may declare a `state` module that the workbench mounts once for the shown project ([plugin state](spec/plugins.md#plugin-state)); it registers the plugin's statuses and commands in the application document, reaches its sidecar under the session `state:<plugin>:<project>` whose `root` is the project folder, and stores declared `data` per project in `projects.json` under `plugins` (both hosts accept the field). The new `@soksak/sidecar-files` lists directories inside `root` and rejects paths outside it. The files plugin exposes `files.tree`, `files.bookmarks`, `files.refresh`, `files.tree.toggle`, and `files.bookmarks.add`/`remove`; 파일 트리 draws the rows with toggle, ☆, and 새로 고침 controls, and 북마크 lists bookmarks with 삭제. The tree updates after a toggle or `files.refresh`, not by itself (F13-2-4). Red on the previous build: `e2e/files.test.mjs` failed on both apps with `unknown command files.refresh`; the host workspace tests failed on `plugins`; the plugin-api, workbench, files, and sidecar unit tests failed before their code. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts: `the files sections show the project folder and its bookmarks from the files state` passes 2/2 (native clicks expand a folder, refresh, bookmark a file, and remove it; the bookmark survives a reload).
  - [o] F13-2-4 — P2: Update the file tree when the project folder changes on disk. Acceptance: in both apps, a file created or removed in an expanded directory outside the application appears in or leaves `files.tree` without a `files.refresh`, through a change notification of the files sidecar (spec first in `docs/spec/sidecars.md`). Done: the files sidecar takes `watch` with the expanded paths and sends `{changed: path}` when an entry of a watched directory changes; macOS uses a kqueue `EVFILT_VNODE` filter per directory, and Linux and Windows return `not implemented` ([files sidecar](spec/sidecars.md#files)). The files state watches the project folder and every expanded directory, lists them again on a change, shows a later watch failure in `files.tree.error`, and stops watching on dispose. Red: `e2e/files.test.mjs` failed on the previous build in both apps with `a file created on disk did not appear without a refresh`; the sidecar watch test did not build (`Changed` undefined); the files state test failed. Green: the sidecar watch tests pass 30/30 (`make go-repeat`), the files plugin tests 10/10, and on rebuilt Wails and Tauri hosts `the files sections show the project folder and its bookmarks from the files state` passes 2/2, where a file created and removed on disk appears and leaves without `files.refresh`.
  - [o] F13-2-5 — P1: Draw the 파일 트리 section as the soksak file tree (github.com/soksak-ai/soksak-plugin-file-tree, `src/tree.tsx` and `src/styles.ts`), which the user chose on 2026-09-26. Acceptance, in both apps: the section renders with `@pierre/trees` (Apache-2.0, its vanilla or web-component API; our plugins do not use React) with file-type icons, selection, and a 4-point scrollbar; tree colors are bound to our theme tokens so every theme and mode applies without a library default showing through; directories load when opened and a disk change reconciles only its directory through the F13-2-4 watch; git status decorations come from the files sidecar and refresh after a change; captures of dark and light in both apps are inspected against the soksak tree.   - [o] V5-8 — P1: Inject named keys through the same physical-key path as letters. `sp_input_key` builds letters and digits from a `CGEvent` with the key code, but builds named keys (Backspace, Enter, Tab, Escape, arrows) with `+[NSEvent keyEventWithType:...]` and fixed characters. Measured on 2026-09-23 in the activation tier: during a Korean 2-Set composition in an AppKit text view, a `CGEvent` Backspace (key code 51) is handled by the input method (`한` becomes `하`), while the injected named Backspace bypasses it and deletes `한`. Window checks that press named keys therefore do not reproduce hardware input. Acceptance: Red in `image_region_ime_test` where the injected Backspace during composition leaves `하` in the AppKit control; named keys without explicit text are built from their key codes; `input_inject_test` and the native suite pass; the window checks that press named keys pass on both rebuilt hosts. Red: the new `image_region_ime_test` check found `''` in the control after `gks` and an injected Backspace. Green: `sp_input_key` builds every key without explicit text from its key code through `CGEvent`, and the control keeps `하`; `input_inject_test` and `make native-test` pass. On the rebuilt hosts the browser, modal, and shell checks pass; the terminal keyboard check failed because the user's selected input source was Korean 2-Set and turned `echo` into `ㄷ쵀`, so the check now selects ABC through the new `session.selectInputSource` and restores the previous source, and it passes on both hosts. Wails `native terminal selection renders and copies` failed once with `selection is empty` and passed on rerun (V5-9). `three terminals survive repeated divider drags` fails on both hosts with 10 of 11 drag extremes recorded; it uses pointer input only and is recorded in F8-6. With physical-key Space the input method now inserts the space itself, so `image_region_ime_test` fails its space checks: that is the Red of F8-16-1.
  - [o] V5-9 — P1: Make `native terminal selection renders and copies through one explicit paste` deterministic. On 2026-09-23 it failed once on the rebuilt Wails host with `invalidParams: selection is empty` and passed on rerun. Acceptance: reproduce the failure through a tracked repeat, report the measured selection state on failure, correct the cause, and pass repeated runs on both hosts. On 2026-09-23 it failed again on the first run after a fresh Wails launch and passed three following runs. Done on 2026-09-24: the cause was the send order of surface module sidecar messages. `sidecar().send` of `surfaceContextRuntime` called the host without waiting for the previous send, and the host can handle calls concurrently, so the sidecar could receive `selection.update` before `selection.start`; page documents already used `orderedSidecar`. The failure report now includes the screen before and after the selection, the grid size, screen changes during the gesture, and the sidecar rejection reasons from the transcript; this measurement showed `selection update without selection start` followed by `selection is empty`, with no PTY output and no grid change during the gesture. The background session log dropped the `reason` of sidecar error replies; it now reports it. Surface and window sidecar sends now go through one `orderedSidecar` port per sidecar name, and a surface send to another surface is rejected instead of being redirected. The new tracked target `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e run repeat` repeats one window check and stops at the first failure. Red: the unit test `surface sidecar sends reach the host one at a time in send order` failed on the previous code, and on a build without the change the repeat target failed on run 11 of 16 on Wails (Tauri passed 16). Green: on both rebuilt hosts the repeat target passes 32 runs in a row each, and the full window check suite passes 127 of 129; the two failures are the still open V5-12.

Acceptance: format the Rust packages in one change without behavior changes, add a formatting check for every Rust package to a required gate, and make an injected unformatted line fail it. Done on 2026-09-24: the 20 Rust files of the root workspace (`apps/tauriv2`, `packages/host/tauriv2`) and the `sidecars` workspace (`vt-core`, `vt-alacritty`) were formatted with `cargo fmt --all` in one change without behavior changes. The new target `make rust-format-check` runs `cargo fmt --all --check` on both workspaces, and the required gate `make native-test` runs it first. Red: an unformatted line added to `vt-core/src/encoding.rs` makes the check fail with exit code 2, and removing it makes the check pass.   - [o] V5-11 — P1: Correct `resizing preserves document geometry and native input`. On 2026-09-23 it failed on both rebuilt hosts: after the resize, `host.hit` at a terminal surface point returned `{kind: "page"}` instead of `{kind: "surface", surface}`. The same failure occurred on a build without the F8-6-1 change. Acceptance: identify whether the hit test or the check is wrong, correct it, and pass the case on both rebuilt hosts. Done on 2026-09-23: the check assumed the earlier structure, and both hosts kept a branch that could not be reached. Since surface modules mount into the app DOM, a logical surface is an `SPSurfaceHost` view, not a WKWebView. DOM surfaces and image regions take DOM input (an image region always has `input: "dom"`), so the main page owns their points. On both rebuilt applications the centers of the shell surface and the terminal image region returned `{kind: "page"}` and the browser document region returned `{kind: "document", …}`. The Wails hit test takes the nearest WKWebView as the owner, so its `surface` branch could not be reached, and the Tauri view-chain lookup never reached a surface view because surface views take no input. `host.hit` in the [exposure specification](spec/exposure.md) now defines only `page`, `document`, and `native`; the `surface` branch and schema value are removed from both hosts; the window check requires a DOM surface point to be `page`. On both rebuilt hosts the case and the browser and modal e2e files that use `host.hit` (22 cases) pass, and `make native-test` passes with 207 of 207 contract cases on both hosts.

Measured on 2026-09-23 after the check was changed to report the item it could not measure: Wails frame 20 of 191 had the rail ink `[69,74,146]` where the sidebar card background belongs, and Tauri frame 78 of 190 had no rail ink right of the sidebar edge. The card DOM is drawn only through the layout commit hook after native preparation; that the DOM still leads indicates that its rendering reaches the screen outside the window's open layer transaction, which is not yet verified. This is the drag lag that F8-6 diagnoses; this item closes with it. Done on 2026-09-24 with F8-6: the rail measurement failure was the rail outline leading the cards. The case passes 10 tracked repeats on both rebuilt hosts and now also limits the drag delay.

Acceptance: report the measured inspector state on failure, identify whether WebKit attaches the inspector differently on the current OS or the check is wrong, correct it, and pass repeated activation-tier runs. Done on 2026-09-24: two defects in the check caused the failure. It requested activation but waited by running the run loop without dispatching events, so the activation event never arrived and the application stayed inactive (measured: app active 0, key 0); it now dispatches events while it waits and asserts that its window becomes the key window of the active application. The inspected 400×300 surface was below the height WebKit requires before it attaches an inspector (about 333 points), so the inspector stayed in its own window; the surface is now 600×450. The check also declared private WebKit API itself; the declarations moved to `native/darwin/src/private/webkit.h`, and a failure now reports the inspector window, the test window, and the activation state. Acceptance: find a current case in which WebKit or AppKit moves a surface, make the check fail without the hold, or remove the hold and the specification sentence when no such case exists. Done on 2026-09-24: no current case moves a surface. Only the Wails main document web view enables the Web Inspector; document regions and surface hosts enable none, and attaching an inspector resizes the inspected web view inside its own superview, not the surface hosts beside it. An added check that tried to attach an inspector to a document region confirmed that it does not attach (no inspector window), and the surface check passed with the hold disabled. Acceptance: report the responder chain state on failure, find why the click does not focus the document in the active window, correct it, and pass repeated runs with the application active and inactive on both hosts. Done on 2026-09-24: `host.window` now reports the window's first responder `{class, owner, surface, document}`. In a failed run the first responder was the window itself (`TaoWindow`), not the document or any view. A click that focuses a browser document in an unfocused card also changes the card focus, and the placement that follows hides the surface until its DOM is drawn; AppKit moves the first responder out of a view that is hidden, so the document lost the focus it had just received. A surface hidden by a placement now records its keyboard owner and the responder AppKit chose instead, and returns the focus to the owner when it is shown again unless the focus moved meanwhile. Red: `surface_host_test` reported `NSWindow` as the first responder after a hide and show, `window_facts_test` failed without the responder facts, and the window check failed in 1 of 20 runs (Tauri frontmost) and 1 of 30 runs (applications inactive). Green on rebuilt hosts: 30 of 30 runs with both applications inactive; 30 of 30 on Wails and on Tauri after activating each through System Events, with another application frontmost again when each run ended, so activation for the whole run is not established. The check now waits for `host.window.presented` before `host.hit`; the failure it removed is registered as V5-17-1. Two runs stopped with 1007 when AppKit reported a nonzero `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` mask; its source was not identified, the F8-21-1 path.
  - [o] V5-17-1 — P2: Establish whether a click during a placement preparation reaches the page instead of the native surface under it. On 2026-09-24 `browser document focus and isolation` failed once on Tauri with `host.hit` returning `{kind: "page"}` at the click point right after `host.window` reported the focused document visible; the check now waits for `host.window.presented` before it measures. A preparation hides the surfaces until the DOM is drawn, so input in that interval can go to the app DOM. Acceptance: measure how long a surface stays hidden during a divider drag and a card focus, reproduce a pointer press in that interval through a tracked check, and either show that the press reaches its surface or correct the routing. Done on 2026-09-24 by correcting the routing at its cause. Measured with the host transcript: a card focus sent `syncSurfaces` with every surface hidden, 3.3 ms later with the surfaces shown at their new rectangles, and `presentSurfaces` 18.5 ms after the first; the surfaces were hidden for 4–6 ms on Wails and 6–11 ms per card focus on Tauri. Because the preparation stays in the window's open transaction, the screen still showed the surfaces while AppKit's hit test already skipped the hidden views, so a press there went to the page. `publishAhead` sent `visible: false` for every surface although the specification hides only surfaces without a matching future slot; it now keeps a surface whose future slot was measured visible at its new rectangle and hides only the others, with `visible` always a boolean. The native surfaces specification states the rule and its reason. Red: the compositor test failed with `native preparation keeps a surface whose future slot was measured visible at its new rectangle`, and the new window check `a card focus keeps a shown document visible while its placement is prepared` found a placement request that hid the shown browser surface on both hosts. Green: the workbench tests pass (79); on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the new check passes, `native content, cards, and the sidebar rail stay aligned` and `shell divider drag does not leave a white surface frame`, which compare native and DOM geometry frame by frame, pass at a load average of 22, and all browser checks passed in the first of three tracked runs; the later runs stopped at 1007 after AppKit reported a nonzero `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` mask; the report does not identify its source.

  - [o] V5-19 — P2: Make `scripts/verify-vt-recovery.mjs` deterministic. On 2026-09-24 it failed once during `make native-test` with `endpoint file .../services/vt-alacritty/endpoint.json timed out after 5000ms`, while both check applications were running; three standalone runs right after passed in 170–212ms. Acceptance: reproduce through a tracked repeat target, report the measured service state on failure (process, log, and whether the service started), correct the cause, and pass repeated runs. Progress on 2026-09-24: the service loads the default font (`service.rs` line 196) before it writes `endpoint.json` (line 226), so the first CoreText call of a new process, which F8-19 measured at 2.07–4.47 s, lies inside the 5 s endpoint wait. The script now prints `service_endpoint_ms` on every run and, on a timeout, reports the service pid, its exit code or signal, and the load average next to the service stderr. Eight runs right after measured 23–46 ms at a load average of 5.4–12.4, so the slow first font creation did not occur. Remaining: reproduce a slow endpoint through a tracked repeat and decide with F8-19 whether the font load stays before the endpoint. Done on 2026-09-25: the cause was the check, not the service. The terminal runtime specification lets the service load the default font before it publishes the endpoint, and the host waits for the ready line without a time limit; the script instead polled `endpoint.json` every 20 ms for 5 s, which a first CoreText call of 2.07–4.47 s (F8-19) could exceed. The script now waits for the service's ready line on standard output, as the host does, with a 60 s guard that reports the service pid, exit code or signal, partial output, and load average, reports an exit before the ready line with its status, and requires `endpoint.json` to equal the ready line. Red: the tracked test `VT recovery waits for a ready line that the service writes after several seconds` runs the script against a service that publishes its endpoint after 6 s and failed with `endpoint file … timed out after 5000ms`. Green: that test now reaches the connection stage after `service_endpoint_ms` of at least 6000, `make node-repeat FILE=scripts/test/vt-recovery.test.mjs NAME="VT recovery" COUNT=5` passed 5 of 5 runs, the real-service test passed 20 of 20 runs at a load average of about 20, and `make native-test` measured `service_endpoint_ms=1390` and passed, together with `pnpm test` and the structural checks.

The Wails host sets no application menu, so Wails uses `DefaultApplicationMenu`, whose View menu has Reload, Force Reload, Actual Size, Zoom In, and Zoom Out; the layout and native surfaces do not follow a webview zoom, and a reload replaces the main page. Green: the host test passes, `window_facts_test` checks the menu facts, and on rebuilt hosts the window check passed 5 tracked repeats and after a restart on both hosts. macOS adds its own F full-screen item to a View menu, so the check requires that every View item is a full screen item. Command `+` now does nothing until F12 provides the text size commands.

  - [o] V5-23 — P1: Deliver native keys to the focused terminal in `native keyboard edits and executes independently in three terminals` on Tauri. On 2026-09-24 the check failed in the full default run and alone on the rebuilt Tauri host: each terminal received the focus click and reported its image region focused, the typed keys were sent, and the screen stayed `sh-3.2$`. Wails passed. Measured on 2026-09-24: with the Tauri application made frontmost through System Events the check passed; with it inactive, a temporary record showed each injected key reaching `sp_input_key` with the window not key and `SPImageRegion` as first responder, while `-[SPImageRegion keyDown:]` was never called, so `-[NSWindow sendEvent:]` on the inactive Tao window does not deliver the key to its first responder. Reverting the F8-6, F8-21-1, V5-17, or V5-18 changes one at a time did not change the result. Acceptance: report the key delivery state (key window, first responder, input source, sidecar input) on failure, find where the keys are lost, correct it, and pass repeated runs on both hosts. Progress on 2026-09-24: the keys are not lost in Tao. Temporary records in `sp_input_key` and `SPImageRegion` showed each key reaching `-[SPImageRegion keyDown:]`, then `-[NSTextInputContext handleEvent:]` returning YES without calling `insertText:`, with the ABC layout selected, the application inactive, and no current input context. In Wails the same call inserted the text synchronously. After Wails was made frontmost once through System Events and then deactivated, its check failed in the same way, and no text input client in the process received `insertText:`, `setMarkedText:`, or `doCommandBySelector:`. The input context therefore passes a character key to the input method, which does not answer outside the key window of the active application once the application has been active; Tauri activates itself at launch and Wails passed only because it had never been active. No public interface makes the input method answer an inactive window. The terminal image region now reports a character key that its input context left unanswered outside that key window as the error `input method did not answer native keyCode=<code> outside the key window of the active application` instead of dropping it, the exposure specification states the limit, and the keyboard check moved to the activation tier as `e2e/activation/terminal-keyboard.test.mjs`. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: both checks passed once; in a tracked repeat the second run failed on both hosts with `selection coordinates are outside the terminal grid: 30.06640625,279.171875`, registered as V5-27. Remaining: pass tracked repeats on both hosts after V5-27. Done on 2026-09-24 after V5-27: on rebuilt Wails and Tauri at commit 4964cb0, `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e verify:activation` passed all activation checks, and a tracked repeat of `native keyboard edits and executes independently in three terminals` and `a character key to a terminal outside the key window reports an input method error` passed 5 of 5 runs.

Done on 2026-09-24 with a corrected cause: the check puts a fixture `cargo` first on `PATH`, so nothing compiles; the measured time is the start of `pnpm test` itself, which runs beside the other packages' tests, including the Rust compilation, in `pnpm test`. Progress on 2026-09-24: the press step now reports the down and up result codes and the AppKit-reported mouse-button mask before and after, and fails at once when the press is not delivered instead of waiting 10 seconds; the test prints the window's occlusion state at start. Measured: the test orders its window to the back so that it takes no focus, and passing runs also start occluded (`window occlusion visible at start: 0`), so occlusion does not separate failures from passes. During V5-6-1 the step failed once in `make -C native/darwin test` with the load average about 15, and a tracked repeat then failed once at `scrollBy` (`the event was not handled within 10 seconds`, run 4 of 10, load average 15.1); three later rounds of `make -C native/darwin repeat TEST=webview_geometry_test COUNT=10` passed 30 of 30 runs. On 2026-09-25 `make -C native/darwin repeat TEST=webview_geometry_test COUNT=40` passed 40 of 40 runs at a load average of about 14–20. Remaining: identify the cause from the result codes of a reproduced failure. Done on 2026-09-25: the press failure coincides with AppKit reporting a nonzero `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` mask; the report does not identify its source. `make -C native/darwin repeat TEST=webview_geometry_test COUNT=100` failed at run 1 with `press on the last device pixel delivered at scale 2 (down 4, up 4; AppKit button mask before 1, after 1)`: result code 4 is `SP_INPUT_BUTTON_HELD`, which `input_inject.m` returns without delivering a press or release while `NSEvent.pressedMouseButtons` is not zero (F8-21-1), and AppKit reported button mask 1 before and after the step; the source was not identified. Before the progress change the test then waited 10 seconds for a press WebKit never received, which produced the recorded `WebKit did not answer within 10 seconds (step: pressLastPixel …)`. AppKit reported mask 0 shortly afterward. The check fails at once with this report, which is the specified behavior when injected input encounters a nonzero AppKit button mask. The `scrollBy` failure recorded in this item is a different step and is registered as V5-25-1.
  - [o] V5-25-1 — P2: Establish why `webview_geometry_test` once failed at `scrollBy` with `the event was not handled within 10 seconds` (2026-09-24, run 4 of 10, load average 15.1). Acceptance: the failure reports the AppKit-reported mouse-button mask, whether a wheel event was dequeued and sent, and the last `scrollTop`, and the cause is identified through a tracked repeat. Done on 2026-09-25: the source-less wheel event inherited Shift from the HID-system modifier state; the report does not identify who or what set that state. The failure now reports every wheel event it dequeued and sent with its deltas, event and HID-system modifier flags, phases, window, location, and hit view, the AppKit-reported mouse-button mask, the page visibility, animation frames, scroll size, and the last `scrollTop`. Tracked repeats of `make -C native/darwin repeat TEST=webview_geometry_test` reproduced it four times in about 250 runs at a load average of 15–42; the last report was `delta -3,0 modifiers 0x20104 HID-system modifiers 0x20104 … hit WKWebView` with `scrollTop` 0: the wheel event created without a source inherited Shift from the HID-system modifier state, and AppKit turned the vertical three-line scroll into a horizontal one, which does not move a document without horizontal overflow. A measurement that turned off WebKit's window occlusion detection kept the page visible but still failed, so page visibility is not the cause and that change was removed. The check now sets the event flags to 0; `make -C native/darwin repeat` then passed 315 runs without a scroll failure, interrupted three times by the V5-25 press report of a held mouse button, and `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, and the structural checks pass. The injection path in `input_inject.m` also creates wheel events without setting their flags; this is registered as V5-37.

Both hosts reported the same point, which is not the click point (the view center), so the move came from a fixed position, consistent with the pointer location WebKit reports when the window becomes key; the terminal module starts a selection on any `pointermove` of the captured pointer whether or not the left button is held. Acceptance: a move without the left button does not start or update a selection; a point inside the region beyond the last full row or column maps to the nearest cell, and a point outside the region stays an error; the terminal runtime specification states both; plugin and sidecar tests fail before the change; a window check drags into the bottom padding on both rebuilt hosts. Done on 2026-09-24 with a revised first criterion. The terminal module cannot tell the move apart by its buttons: WebKit reads the pressed buttons from the system, so a synthetic drag also reaches the document with `buttons` 0, and a module that ignored such moves failed `a native drag over blank terminal cells ends without a surface error` and `native terminal selection renders and copies through one explicit paste` on both hosts; that change was removed. The move came from the system pointer: the first tracked run enlarged the window over the pointer, and the key window of the active application receives the pointer location as a move. `host.window` now reports `pointer`, the system pointer location in screen coordinates, and before activating the application every activation-tier check moves its window beside the pointer through `keepPointerOutside` in `e2e/app.mjs`, first narrowing the window when it fits on neither side; the exposure specification states the move and the placement. The sidecar now accepts a point inside the region past the last whole column or row and maps it to the nearest cell, and reports a point outside the region as `selection coordinates are outside the terminal region`; the terminal runtime specification states it. Red: `test_selection_in_the_region_padding_selects_the_nearest_cell` found the padding point rejected, and the new window check `a native drag into the padding below the last row selects to the last row` failed on both hosts with `invalidParams: selection coordinates are outside the terminal grid: 28,282.75`. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: the sidecar and plugin tests pass, the padding check and the two selection checks pass, `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e verify:activation` passes all activation checks, and a tracked repeat of the two keyboard checks passed 5 of 5 runs.

The specification says a tab that left the layout keeps its last factor until it is disposed, so the surface context now records the factor when the mount starts, while the tab is in the layout, and `read` returns the current factor or the last one; it throws only when neither exists. The surface module tests register the plane's factor function in their setup, as the plane does before any mount. The page diagnostic that showed the stack was temporary and removed. Red: `a tab that leaves the layout during its mount reads the factor it was mounted with` failed with `surface tab-leaving is not in the layout`, the message of the window failure. The workbench sends `sidecarsRetain` with the surfaces of every layout; both hosts add every surface this process has sent and send one `retain` request to each running or published persistent service, which closes the owner's other sessions and answers `retained` with the closed count. A service without a running process or endpoint file is not started. Red: `scripts/verify-vt-recovery.mjs`, run by `make native-test` against the service binary, now leaves an orphan surface without a closed notice, restarts the application process, and requires `retain` to close exactly that session; against the service built from the previous `protocol.rs` the restarted worker received no reply and the orphan session stayed. The real-input check `an injected wheel keeps its own modifier flags while HID system reports Shift` posts a Shift key down through the HID system, injects a vertical wheel over a terminal with `input.pointer`, and requires the page's wheel event to carry no modifiers and the history to scroll.   - [o] V5-39 — P0: Scroll the terminal scrollback with the wheel.
