Acceptance: the specification defines wheel behavior for the primary screen, alternate screen, and mouse-reporting modes, and a real-input check on both hosts scrolls the scrollback and returns to the bottom on input. Done on 2026-09-25: the terminal had no wheel handling at all, and the engine's screen mapped grid rows to screen rows without the viewport offset. The terminal runtime specification now defines wheel input: the page turns DOM wheel deltas into whole lines at the pointer cell and sends `scroll`; the sidecar writes wheel button events when mouse reporting is on (SGR, UTF-8, or the default encoding through the new `encode_wheel`), cursor keys on the alternate screen with alternate scroll on, and otherwise moves the primary viewport through the scrollback; input returns the viewport to the newest output; `terminal.session` reports `scrollback: {offset, history}` from the screen event. The alacritty engine maps screen rows, the cursor (hidden outside the viewport), selection points, and inline-image rows through the viewport offset. `scroll_to_newest` moves only a scrolled viewport, because `scroll_display` emits `mouse.cursor.dirty` even without movement, which broke `a native drag over blank terminal cells ends without a surface error` in the first build. Red: the engine test `the_viewport_scrolls_through_the_scrollback_and_returns_to_the_newest_output`, the serve contracts for the three wheel rules and invalid scrolls, the encoding test `wheel_events_follow_the_mouse_encoding`, the plugin test `wheel input is sent as whole scroll lines at the pointer cell and the screen reports scrollback`, and the real-input check `a real wheel scrolls the scrollback and a key returns to the newest output` (no viewport movement within 20 s on either host) failed before the change. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: the real-input wheel check passed three runs per host (a five-line wheel event moved the viewport 12 lines after the application's event monitor converted it), `a real wheel on the alternate screen scrolls the program instead of the scrollback` scrolls `less` with the scrollback offset at 0, the real-input suite passes 14 of 14, `e2e/terminal.test.mjs` and `e2e/terminal-font.test.mjs` pass 58 of 58, plugin tests 91 of 91, and the sidecar tests, `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, and the structural checks pass. A wheel over the primary screen still forwards alacritty's `mouse.cursor.dirty` renderer event, which the page records as unsupported; V5-51 decides it.
  - [o] V5-40 — P0: Clear a terminal selection with a click. After a drag selection, a real click at another point leaves the selection shown (reproduced on Tauri on 2026-09-25). Acceptance: a real-input check on both hosts shows that a click clears the rendered selection pixels and `terminal.session` state; Red before the fix. Done on 2026-09-25: the cause was my change for F6.3-11 (2026-09-23), which stopped a click without movement from sending anything to the sidecar so that a focus click would not report `selection is empty`; the specification later made an empty release a normal gesture that clears the selection, but a click still sent nothing, so the previous selection stayed. On Wails a click passed only because WebKit reported a small move between press and release. A press and release without movement now starts and ends an empty selection at the pressed cell, and the terminal runtime specification states it. Red: the real-input check `a real click clears a terminal selection` failed on Tauri with `the sidecar did not answer the release of a real click after a selection`, and the plugin test `a terminal click clears the selection with an empty selection at the pressed cell` failed before the change. Green: the plugin tests pass 74 of 74, the real-input check passes on rebuilt Wails and Tauri (the selected cell returns to the background colour and the copied text stays `SELECTME`), and `e2e/terminal.test.mjs` passes 50 of 50. The activation keyboard check failed on both hosts with and without this change; a build without 3ec65e0 passed it 10 of 10, and that regression is registered as V5-46. `pnpm test` and the structural checks pass.
  - [o] V5-41 — P0: Copy a terminal selection to the system clipboard reliably. The user reported that a drag selection did not reach the clipboard and could not be pasted into another application; one posted drag on 2026-09-25 did copy. Command+C is not defined for the terminal. Acceptance: the specification defines Command+C for a selection; a real-input check on both hosts drags, copies, and reads the general pasteboard as another application would, in repeated runs, with the measured cause of the reported failure. Done on 2026-09-25: the reported failure was measured with real input. A drag selection did reach the general pasteboard, and the following Command+C emptied it: the key went to the terminal region's `NSTextView` `copy:`, which copies the empty selection of the input-method document. A user who selects and then presses Command+C therefore had nothing to paste in another application. The region now answers `copy:` by reporting a `copy` action and keeps Copy enabled; the terminal module runs the new declared command `terminal.copy`, which sends a `copy` request; the sidecar answers with a `copy` event carrying the current selection text from the new engine method `selection_text`, or with `copied: false` when there is no selection, which leaves the clipboard unchanged. The terminal runtime specification states the rule. Red: the real-input check `a real drag copies the selection and Command+C copies it again` failed on both hosts with `the pasteboard holds null`, and the native edit-action case, the plugin test `the region's copy action runs terminal.copy and writes the sidecar's selection text once`, the engine test `the_current_selection_text_is_readable_until_the_selection_is_cleared`, and the serve contract `a_copy_request_sends_the_current_selection_text_or_reports_none` failed before the change. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: the real-input copy check and `a real click clears a terminal selection` passed three runs per host, `e2e/terminal.test.mjs` passes 54 of 54, plugin tests 90 of 90, and the sidecar tests, `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, and the structural checks pass. A real-input case for Command+C without a selection is not included, because the page has no status to wait on for the `copied: false` answer; the contract and plugin tests cover it.
  - [o] V5-42 — P0: Paste with Command+V through `terminal.paste` without delay. No shortcut binds Command+V to `terminal.paste`; the key goes to the menu, the transcript shows no `clipboardRead`, and a posted Command+V took 3.4 s and 4.0 s to show the text on Tauri on 2026-09-25. Acceptance: Command+V runs the declared command, the path and each stage time are measured, and a real-input check on both hosts shows the text within 100 ms of the key at a low load. Done on 2026-09-25: Command+V went to the Edit menu's `paste:`, which reached the terminal region's `NSTextView` superclass. It inserted the pasteboard text into the input-method document, where it stayed until another key or event committed it, so the text appeared seconds later or only after the next typed character (the user reported the latter; posted Command+V measured 2.6 s, 3.4 s, 4.0 s, 13.1 s, and no text within 20 s). My earlier description of this as a slow paste was wrong. The region now answers `paste:` and `pasteAsPlainText:` by reporting a `paste` action, disables Cut, Select All, and Delete, and the terminal module runs `terminal.paste` for the action through the command registry; `createBinder` returns a `run` that rejects undeclared commands and the plugin page exposes it. The terminal runtime specification states the rule. Red: the native `image_region_test` edit-action cases, the plugin tests `the region's paste action runs terminal.paste once` and `the region reports an unknown action as an input error`, the binder test for `run`, and the real-input check `a real Command+V pastes the clipboard text without another key` (limit 500 ms; no text within 20 s on either host) failed before the change. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri at a load average of about 6–8: the real-input check passed three runs per host, with the text shown 7–15 ms after the key-posting tool returned (165–226 ms including the `osascript` start); plugin tests pass 87 of 87, plugin-api 78 of 78, `make native-test`, `pnpm test`, the structural checks, and `e2e/terminal.test.mjs` (52 of 52) pass.
  - [o] V5-43 — P0: Paste an image with Command+V. The user reported that image paste does not work in the application. Acceptance: a real-input check on both hosts puts a PNG on the pasteboard, presses Command+V, and requires the quoted owned-file path, restoring the previous pasteboard items. Done on 2026-09-25: the cause was the same as V5-42. Command+V reached the `NSTextView` paste of the terminal region, which cannot insert an image, so a clipboard PNG produced nothing. Since V5-42 the region reports a `paste` action that runs `terminal.paste`, which reads text, file URLs, then PNG and inserts the quoted path of the owned file. The real-input check `a real Command+V pastes a clipboard image as a quoted owned path` puts a 1x1 PNG on the general pasteboard, clicks the terminal, posts Command+V, and requires a quoted path in `<config-dir>/clipboard/` whose file holds exactly the PNG bytes; it restores every pasteboard item type and removes the file. Red: a build whose region did not answer `paste:` failed the check on both hosts (no quoted path within 20 s). Green: on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the check passed three runs per host, and `pnpm test` and the structural checks pass.
  - [o] V5-44 — P1: Verify terminal protocol support in the running application. V5-6-6 compares the specification's OSC table with the engine inventory; no check runs each supported CSI and OSC sequence in the application and inspects the result. Acceptance: for each sequence the specification lists as supported, a window check writes it through the PTY and verifies the screen, mode, or event result on both hosts, and unsupported sequences are listed as such. Progress on 2026-09-25 through V5-51-6: `e2e/terminal-protocols.test.mjs` writes every implemented CSI and OSC sequence through the PTY and verifies its screen, cursor, mode, reply, or event result on both hosts, the vendor OSC sequences have their own window checks, and the specification's table lists each unsupported row with its reason. Remaining: the real-input keypad check of V5-51-6-5. Done on 2026-09-25 with V5-51-6.
  - [o] V5-45 — P0: Deliver host diagnostic input through the same dispatch as a person's input. `sp_input_key` sends key events to `-[NSWindow sendEvent:]`, which skips the menu key equivalents that `-[NSApplication sendEvent:]` runs, so an injected Command+V or Command+C never reaches the Edit menu; `sp_input_pointer` calls `mouseDown:`, `mouseDragged:`, and `mouseUp:` on the hit view instead of dispatching the event through the window. Acceptance: injected keys and pointer events go through `-[NSApplication sendEvent:]` with the same event fields as HID events, the exposure specification states it, and the window checks for Command+C, Command+V, selection, and click run with both injected and real input on both hosts with the same result. Done on 2026-09-25 for keys; the pointer part is registered as V5-45-1. `sp_input_key` now returns an `sp_input_result`: a key to the key window goes through `-[NSApplication sendEvent:]` with the window's number (`CGEventField` 51, listed in the private API inventory), so AppKit runs key equivalents such as Command+C and Command+V as it does for a person's key; a key to a window while another window is the key window returns `SP_INPUT_INACTIVE`, which both hosts report as 1006 `keys reach only the key window of the active application`; without a key window the key goes to the window's `-[NSWindow sendEvent:]`, which the checks that do not activate the application use. A first version that sent every key through the main menu's `performKeyEquivalent:` swallowed Backspace in Wails through the Edit menu's `\b` Delete item, while a real HID Backspace deleted the character on both hosts, so the key-window path leaves that decision to AppKit. The exposure specification states the rules, and the activation-tier check `a key to a window that is not the key window is rejected instead of delivered` replaces the check that expected the image region's input-method error for such a key. Red: the new activation-tier check `injected Command+C and Command+V run the Edit menu like a person's keys` failed on both hosts with the previous `-[NSWindow sendEvent:]` path (`an injected Command+C did not copy the selection`). Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: that check and the activation keyboard checks pass 12 of 12, the input-method checks pass 4 of 4, `modal`, `browser`, `shell`, and `terminal` window checks pass 85 of 86 with the remaining divider-drag check failing on a capture that received no frame and passing when run again, `make -C native/darwin repeat-suite COUNT=3` passes, and `pnpm test`, `make native-test`, and the structural checks pass. One earlier `make native-test` run failed `input_inject_test` waiting for a keydown and one `pnpm test` run failed `lock_is_single_instance_and_releases` at a load average above 19; the native test passed 3 of 3 alone and within three suite repeats, and the lock test 20 of 20 alone.
  - [o] V5-45-1 — P1: Decide how diagnostic pointer input relates to a person's pointer. A person's click on an inactive window reaches the application through the window server, which first activates the application and makes the window key; window checks must not activate the application. On 2026-09-25 dispatching injected presses, drags, and releases through `-[NSApplication sendEvent:]` to inactive windows did not activate the application but failed 20 window checks, because AppKit and WebKit handle a click in an inactive window differently. The exposure specification keeps delivery to the view under the point and relies on the real-input tier for the window-server path. Acceptance: the user's decision between that rule and pointer checks that activate the application, and the specification and checks following it. Done on 2026-09-25: the user chose to keep the current rule. Diagnostic presses, drags, and releases go to the view under the point without activating the application, and the real-input tier (`e2e/real/`, V5-38) checks the window-server path of a person's pointer; the exposure specification states the rule and the measurement that 20 window checks failed when injected pointer events were dispatched through `-[NSApplication sendEvent:]` to inactive windows.
  - [o] V5-46 — P0: Restore native keyboard focus for a clicked terminal. 3ec65e0 (V5-17-1) keeps measured surfaces visible while their placement is prepared; since then a click on a terminal gives the card focus border but not native keyboard focus, so the border and the focused cursor show different terminals (reported by the user on 2026-09-25), and the activation check `native keyboard edits and executes independently in three terminals` fails on both hosts with `terminal 1 did not receive keyboard focus in the active window`. A build with only that line reverted passed the activation checks 10 of 10. Acceptance: the measured cause, a fix that keeps the V5-17-1 check `a card focus keeps a shown document visible while its placement is prepared` passing, and the activation keyboard check and a real-input check of the card border and focused cursor passing on both hosts. Done on 2026-09-25: the cause was an ordering fault in `plane.js` that 3ec65e0 exposed. A press on another card ran `core.card.focus` and recorded the surface to focus only after the command returned; the render that the command starts calls `takeSurfaceFocus()` when its presentation completes. While preparation hid every surface, the presentation finished after the record; with 3ec65e0 it finished first, so every recorded focus waited for the next render and each click focused the previously clicked terminal. A probe that clicked three terminals measured the responder one click behind (`click tab-msqamv` → `SPImageRegion/tab-bb265b`). The press now records the target surface before it runs the command and removes it if the command fails. Red: the new window check `a click gives native focus to the clicked terminal and its card` failed on both hosts with `did not become the native first responder after its click`, and the activation keyboard check failed on both hosts. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: the window check passes and `pnpm -F @soksak/e2e run repeat` passed it 10 of 10, the real-input check `a real click gives native focus to the clicked terminal and its card` passes, the activation keyboard check passes 10 of 10, the V5-17-1 check `a card focus keeps a shown document visible while its placement is prepared` passes, `terminal.test.mjs`, `browser.test.mjs`, and `shell.test.mjs` pass 70 of 70, and `pnpm test` and the structural checks pass. I committed 3ec65e0 without running the activation keyboard check, which would have shown this regression.
  - [o] V5-47 — P2: Clear the input-method error that the activation check leaves on a terminal. `a character key to a terminal outside the key window reports an input method error` sets the session error `input method did not answer native keyCode=… outside the key window of the active application` on a terminal of the check window and leaves it shown after the check; the user saw it on 2026-09-25. Acceptance: the check resolves or removes the terminal it used, and the next check starts with no session error. Done on 2026-09-25 through V5-45 (`07011b6`): the check that left the error no longer exists. Its replacement, `a key to a window that is not the key window is rejected instead of delivered`, rejects the key before delivery with `keys reach only the key window of the active application` and requires the terminal session to have no error after the rejected key; it passed in the V5-45 activation runs (keyboard checks 12 of 12). No activation run was made on later code for this item.
  - [o] V5-48 — P0: Keep the terminal content and cursor consistent after a card resize. On 2026-09-25 the user resized a card holding a terminal whose prompt line wrapped; afterwards the line showed characters after the cursor that the shell does not hold, and the cursor could not move over them. Acceptance: a tracked check that reproduces the state by resizing a terminal with a wrapped input line, the measured cause in the engine reflow or the cursor mapping, a fix, and the check passing on both hosts. Progress on 2026-09-25: a tracked probe resized a terminal with a wrapped `/bin/sh` input line from 122 to 60 to 122 columns and left `…WORD11-ABCDEFGHIJD05-ABCDEFGHIJ WORDsh-3.2$ …` on the line; the same steps with `/bin/sh` produced the same text in another terminal, and zsh left a clean line in both terminals. The engine reflow matches that terminal; the residue comes from bash 3.2 redrawing its input line after `SIGWINCH` from the row count of the old width while the terminal has already reflowed the line. The sidecar also started `/bin/sh` instead of the account's login shell, which is registered as V5-50; that change does not resolve this item, because a bash input line still leaves the residue. The remaining work is terminal-side handling that does not depend on the shell's redraw, such as clearing the prompt region on resize from OSC 133 marks with injected shell integration (V5-51). Done on 2026-09-25 through V5-51-1: the shells redraw after `SIGWINCH` by moving up the cursor row count of the previous width while the terminal has reflowed the line, which left the reflowed rows beside the redrawn prompt (another terminal shows the same for bash 3.2). With shell integration the engine clears the prompt's logical line and places the cursor where the shell expects it; `tests/shell_redraw_test.rs` reproduces the resize with real zsh and bash, and the window check passes on both hosts for zsh and bash. A shell without integration keeps the previous behavior.
  - [o] V5-49 — P1: Restore the user's pasteboard after every window check. `native terminal selection renders and copies through one explicit paste` and the PNG paste check write the general pasteboard and do not restore every item type; on 2026-09-25 the user's clipboard held `SELECTION-CLIP-123` from a check. Acceptance: every window check that writes the pasteboard saves and restores all item types, and a check fails when the pasteboard after the run differs from before it. Done on 2026-09-25. The harness (`open` in `e2e/app.mjs`) now saves every item type of the general pasteboard before each window check and, after the check's other cleanup, writes them back and fails the check when the pasteboard then differs from before it. The pasteboard helpers moved from `e2e/real/hid.mjs` to `e2e/pasteboard.mjs` and pass items to AppKit through a file, because a pasteboard image can exceed the command-argument limit; the per-check restores in the real-input and activation checks and the text-only restore of the PNG paste check were removed. Red: with the harness check but without the restore, `an explicit PNG paste writes an owned image and inserts its quoted path` failed on both hosts (`public.png instead of public.utf8-plain-text`) and `native terminal selection renders and copies through one explicit paste` failed on Tauri. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: both checks passed three runs per host, the real-input tier passed 38 of 38, and a full window run passed 162 of 167 with the five failures being the divider drags that V5-63 records, which passed after that fix. The activation-tier file changed only its imports and was not run, because activation runs need the user's approval.
  - [o] V5-50 — P1: Start the account's login shell in a terminal session. The sidecar started `/bin/sh` for every session, so a user's zsh configuration never loaded. Acceptance: the terminal setting `terminal.shell` (`login` by default, or an absolute executable path) selects the shell, the sidecar resolves `login` from the user database and starts the shell as a login shell, invalid values are explicit errors without another shell, background sessions receive the value through `background.settings`, window checks start a tracked check shell, and the checks pass on both rebuilt hosts. Done on 2026-09-25: the sidecar started `/bin/sh` for every session, so the account's zsh never loaded. The plugin now declares `terminal.shell` (`login` by default, or an absolute executable path); the terminal page sends it as `shell` in its `open` request and the workbench sends it for background sessions through the new manifest field `background.settings`, which `validateManifest` restricts to declared settings. The sidecar requires the field, resolves `login` from `getpwuid` in `platform/darwin/account.rs`, rejects relative, missing, and non-executable paths, and starts the resolved shell as a login shell through `open_shell` (`argv[0]` `-name`, `SHELL` its path). `diagnostics.fixture` accepts a `settings` object applied with the defaults in one change, and window checks start the tracked `e2e/check-shell`, which runs `/bin/sh` as a non-login shell, because the login `/bin/sh` prompt `host:dir user$` wrapped in narrow cards and broke `multiple terminals keep fixed cells during a divider drag` through the V5-48 redraw residue. `make boundaries` no longer applies the plugin-id rule to sidecars, whose domain words such as `shell` are not plugin references; I had first renamed the request field to avoid that rule and reverted it. Red: `tests/shell_test.rs` did not compile before `resolve_shell` and `open_shell` existed, the plugin tests `a session opens the shell that the shell setting names` and `a session without a shell setting is not opened` failed, the serve contract `an_open_without_a_shell_is_rejected_without_a_session` covers the missing field, and the manifest validation cases failed before `background.settings` existed. Green: sidecar workspace tests, plugin tests 89 of 89, plugin-api 78 of 78, `pnpm test`, `make native-test`, and the structural checks pass; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri `e2e/terminal.test.mjs` and `e2e/terminal-font.test.mjs` pass 56 of 56 (one run of `a native drag over blank terminal cells ends without a surface error` was refused after AppKit reported a nonzero button mask and passed when run again), and the new window check `the login shell setting starts the account's login shell as a login shell` reads `ARGV0=-zsh` on both hosts. The inline-image check failed intermittently at a load average of 11–15 on both this build and the committed build (5 of 12 each), registered as V5-53. This change does not resolve V5-48.
  - [o] V5-51 — P0: Implement the OSC and CSI sequences as fully as the terminal can support them, as the user asked on 2026-09-25. F7 closed an inventory with 11 unsupported OSC rows while `docs/spec/terminal-protocols.md` still lists pending evidence (hyperlinks, font, window and resource operations, graphics, complete color queries), and OSC 133 marks are parsed but not used. Acceptance: each XTerm and vendor selector is implemented or has a stated reason it cannot be, OSC 133 marks with injected shell integration drive prompt handling on resize (V5-48), OSC 8 links open through a user command, and each implemented sequence has a window check on both hosts (V5-44). Progress on 2026-09-25: most implemented and vendor rows only parse sequences into events that the application does not use; the work is split into V5-51-1 to V5-51-6. Done on 2026-09-25: V5-51-1 to V5-51-6 are done; every XTerm and vendor OSC and CSI selector is implemented or rejected with a stated reason in `docs/spec/terminal-protocols.md`, OSC 133 marks drive prompt redraw on resize, OSC 8 links open through a user command, OSC 9 notifications reach the user, and each implemented sequence has a check in the running applications on both hosts.
    - [o] V5-51-1 — P0: Inject shell integration and clear the prompt region on resize from OSC 133 marks. Acceptance: the sidecar starts zsh and bash with integration that emits OSC 133 prompt and command marks without changing the user's configuration files; on a resize while the cursor is in a marked prompt, the engine clears the marked prompt rows before reflow and the shell redraws; a window check resizes a wrapped zsh and bash input line on both hosts and finds no residue (V5-48); shells without integration keep the current behavior, which the specification states. Done on 2026-09-25. The alacritty engine applies each OSC 133 mark at its position in the byte stream, moves the cursor to a new line on `A` when it is not at column 0, keeps a prompt or output state, and rejects `redraw` values other than `0`, `1`, and `last`. On a primary-screen resize in the prompt state it reflows, clears the rows from the first row of the cursor's logical line to the bottom, and places the cursor as many rows below that row as it was before the reflow. It clears after the reflow, not before as this item first stated, because the reflowed logical line and cursor exist only after the reflow. The cursor placement is measured: zsh and readline both move up by the cursor row count of the width they last drew at; with the cursor left in place zsh drew one row low, and with the cursor at the first row bash overwrote the output row above the prompt. The sidecar starts zsh with `ZDOTDIR` pointing at an embedded `.zshenv` and bash with an exported `PROMPT_COMMAND` entry that sources an embedded script. Measured bash 3.2 behavior shaped the script: `--posix` does not read `ENV` unless the shell runs as `sh`; a file read by `source` does not see the `DEBUG` trap and a trap it sets is replaced by an earlier one, so the entry records the trap before `source`; `$(builtin trap -p)` does not show the parent's traps; `LINENO` advances on each line of `PROMPT_COMMAND`, so the first attempt reported an empty input line as a command; an entry ending in a newline broke `PROMPT_COMMAND` extended as macOS recommends (`"$PROMPT_COMMAND; x"`); and a `SIGWINCH` during startup made the trap run for a command after the first `A`. Red: the engine tests `a_resize_in_the_prompt_state_clears_the_cursor_logical_line_to_the_bottom`, `redraw_last_places_the_cursor_as_redraw_1_does`, `redraw_last_scrolls_the_screen_when_the_offset_row_is_below_the_bottom`, `prompt_rows_that_the_reflow_moved_into_the_scrollback_are_cleared`, `a_prompt_start_moves_a_cursor_off_column_zero_to_the_next_line`, and `an_unsupported_redraw_value_is_rejected_without_a_state_change` failed first; `tests/shell_test.rs` failed on each bash defect above; `tests/shell_redraw_test.rs` (real zsh and bash with the engine) showed the overwritten row; and the window check failed on both hosts with `START-` shown three times while the first prompt had reported a command start. Green: engine tests 79 of 79, `shell_test.rs` 7 of 7, `shell_redraw_test.rs` 2 of 2, and the sidecar workspace tests pass; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the window checks `a resize redraws a wrapped zsh input line without rows of the previous width` and the bash case pass with a narrowing, a widening, and a divider drag (three runs per host), `e2e/terminal.test.mjs` and `e2e/terminal-font.test.mjs` pass 62 of 62, and `pnpm test`, `make native-test`, and the structural checks pass. A second size change before the shell redraws can still make the shell draw at a width that is no longer current (measured with bash 3.2 at 30, 45, and 60 columns in the sidecar test); the real-input tier was not run for this change.
    - [o] V5-51-2 — P1: Make OSC 8 hyperlinks usable. Acceptance: linked cells are drawn underlined, the pointer shows a hand over them, Command+click runs a declared command that opens the URI through the host with the user's default handler, the specification states which schemes are opened, and a real-input check on both hosts opens a link. Done on 2026-09-25. The alacritty engine exports each cell's OSC 8 URI as `link`, and the raster draws an underline for linked and SGR 4 cells; the renderer had never drawn underlines, so SGR 4 text and the input-method preedit underline were invisible. Surfaces gained `runtime.links.open`, which calls the new host operation `linkOpen`: both hosts accept absolute `http`, `https`, and `mailto` URLs (host contract cases `links.open.accepts-web-and-mail-schemes` and `links.open.rejects-other-schemes`), the macOS platform opens them through `NSWorkspace` (`native/darwin/src/link.m`), and Windows returns `not implemented on windows`. The terminal page reports the link under the pointer as `link` in `terminal.session`, shows a hand pointer over linked cells, and runs the declared command `terminal.link.open` on a Command-click without starting a selection. Red: the frame test `underlined_and_linked_cells_draw_a_line_below_the_text` and the engine test `osc8_linked_cells_carry_their_uri` did not compile before the `link` field existed, and the three terminal page tests for the hover, the Command-click, and a rejected link failed; the host validation tests, the native `link_test.m`, the link bridge test, and the window checks were written with their implementation and were not run against the earlier code. Green: the sidecar tests, frame tests 17 of 17, plugin-api 80 of 80, terminal plugin 103 of 103, `pnpm test`, and `make native-test` (Wails 217 and Tauri 216 contract cases) pass; on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the window check `OSC 8 linked cells are drawn with an underline and plain cells are not` and the real-input check `a real Command-click on an OSC 8 link opens it through the host and a plain click does not` passed three runs per host, the real-input tier passes 30 of 30, and the structural checks pass. The real-input check opens `https://example.com/soksak-link-check` in the default browser on each run. Of five runs of `e2e/terminal.test.mjs`, `e2e/terminal-font.test.mjs`, and `e2e/commands.test.mjs` at load averages from 8 to 35, two passed 80 of 80; the others failed on the inline-image check (V5-53), a nonzero AppKit-reported mouse-button mask, two Tauri host requests that did not answer in 20 s (V5-54), and in the first run four Wails checks whose messages were not captured and which passed when run again alone.
    - [o] V5-51-3 — P1: Use OSC 0/2 titles and OSC 7 directories. Done on 2026-09-25. Surfaces had no way to name their tab or pass a directory to a new tab, so the workbench gained tab reports ([plugins](spec/plugins.md#tab-reports)): `context.tab.title` sets the title a tab shows instead of its name (`core.grid` reports it as `label`), `context.tab.directory` records the surface's working directory, and a tab created by `+` or a split receives the directory of the card's active tab as `context.origin.directory`. The terminal page reports OSC 0/2 titles unless the new setting `terminal.title` is `name`, removes them on a reset or an empty title, records the OSC 7 directory, and sends `origin.directory` as the new `open` field `directory`; the sidecar decodes OSC 7 URIs into a local `path` (`null` for another machine) and rejects a start directory that is not an existing absolute directory. A terminal that is not split from a terminal with a reported directory still starts in the home directory, not the project folder.

The terminal page passes each OSC 9 notification to it. Red: the tab report, plugin-api, and terminal page tests for notices failed first. Green: those tests and `pnpm test` pass, the window check `an OSC 9 notification from a terminal out of view becomes a tab notice until the tab is in view` passed three runs on rebuilt Wails and Tauri, and `e2e/terminal.test.mjs`, `e2e/terminal-font.test.mjs`, and `e2e/commands.test.mjs` pass 78 of 78.       - [o] V5-51-4-1 — P1: Deliver terminal notifications as macOS notifications. Both applications run as executables outside an application bundle (`bundle.active` is false for Tauri, and Wails builds a plain executable), and `+[UNUserNotificationCenter currentNotificationCenter]` in an unbundled process ends it with an uncaught exception (measured on 2026-09-25 with a minimal Objective-C program). Acceptance: the user decides whether the applications ship as bundles; with bundles, a notification from a terminal out of view requests permission once, posts a system notification whose activation focuses that tab, reports a denied permission as visible state, and a check verifies the delivery on both hosts. Progress on 2026-09-25: the applications run from bundles (`target/debug/soksak-<app>.app`, identifiers `com.soksak.<app>`, sidecars in `Contents/MacOS`, ad hoc signed), because the notification center ends an unbundled process. `native/darwin/src/notifications.m` asks for permission at the first notification, shows banners also while the application is active, replaces a tab's notification with its later notice, removes it with the notice (also when the removal arrives before the post completes), and reports `state`, `posted`, and `activated` events; outside a bundle it fails to start with an explicit error and the hosts do not start. Both hosts add `notify`, `notificationRemove`, `notificationState`, and the diagnostic `diagnostics.notifications`; activating a notification shows and focuses its window and runs `core.tab.select` for its tab. The page posts new or changed tab notices, removes removed ones, reports `core.notifications` as `{authorization, error, posted}`, and ends notice tooltips with `System notifications are turned off for this application.` while permission is denied. Red: `notifications_test` (no module), the host contract cases `notifications.request.accepts-tab-notices` and `notifications.request.rejects-invalid-fields`, the workbench `system-notifications` tests, and the Tauri runtime test `every host command has an argument mapping` (a missing mapping stopped the Tauri page at startup) failed first. Green: those tests, `pnpm test`, `make native-test`, and the structural checks pass; on the bundled applications the checks read `denied` (Wails) and `notDetermined` (Tauri) and reported them explicitly. Remaining: the window check `an OSC 9 notification from a terminal out of view is a system notification until the tab is in view` and the real-input check `pressing a system notification of a terminal out of view selects its tab` on both hosts after the user allows notifications for both applications in System Settings. Done on 2026-09-25: after the user allowed notifications for both applications, the window check `an OSC 9 notification from a terminal out of view is a system notification until the tab is in view` passed on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: the notification center accepted the notification, `diagnostics.notifications` listed it with the tab name as the title and the notice as the body, and it was removed when the tab came into view. The real-input check `pressing a system notification of a terminal out of view selects its tab` finds the banner by its text in the notification center's accessibility tree, presses it with a posted click, and passed on both hosts: the tab became the active tab of the focused card and the window became the key window. Two defects were found and fixed first: the Tauri endpoint did not declare `diagnostics.notifications`, so the request closed the connection, and a running application kept the permission it read before the user changed it until it restarted. The banner lookup reads the notification center's accessibility tree because the banner is drawn inside a full-screen notification center window.

Acceptance: each row of `docs/spec/terminal-protocols.md` is implemented with a test or states its reason, and implemented rows have window checks. Progress on 2026-09-25. OSC 17 and 19 (highlight background and text colors) are set, answered with the request's terminator, reset by OSC 117 and 119, and draw selected cells instead of inverse; OSC 22 maps X cursor font and CSS names to the terminal view's cursor and `terminal.session.pointer`. Green: the engine, vt-core, and plugin tests pass, and on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the OSC window check sets and resets the highlight colors, reads a selected cell without inverse while they are set and inverse after the reset, and reads `terminal.session.pointer`. Remaining: OSC 5, 6, 105, and 106 (special colors for bold, underline, blink, reverse, and italic text) and OSC 13 and 14 (pointer colors through generated pointer images) can be supported and are not implemented. Done on 2026-09-25. OSC 5 sets and answers the special colors 0 (bold), 1 (underline), 3 (reverse), and 4 (italic), OSC 6 and 106 enable them for text with the default foreground, OSC 105 resets them, and the blink color (2) is an explicit error because the grid does not keep the blink attribute. Red: the engine test `osc_special_colors_draw_attributed_text_when_enabled_and_answer_queries` failed before the change. Green: the engine tests pass and the OSC window check sets, queries, and resets a special color and reads the blink rejection on rebuilt Wails and Tauri. Every remaining unsupported row of the table (1, 3, 13, 14, 15, 16, 18, 21, 46, 51, 60–62, I/l/L) states why it cannot apply. The full window run is pending for the final batch.
    - [o] V5-51-6 — P1: Verify each implemented CSI and OSC sequence in the running application on both hosts (includes V5-44). Acceptance: a window check writes each sequence through the PTY and verifies its screen, mode, reply, or event result. Progress on 2026-09-25. `e2e/terminal-protocols.test.mjs` writes sequences through the shell into the PTY and reads the screen cells, `terminal.cursor`, `terminal.session`, `core.grid`, the pasteboard, or the reply, which a shell `read` prints in hexadecimal. `implemented CSI sequences change the screen, the cursor, and the replies` covers `A/B/C/D/G/H/f/s/u`, `E/F`, `3C`, `m`, `S/T` with `r`, `J/K`, `@/P`, `L/M`, `I/Z`, `b`, `?1049h/l`, `?25h/l`, `?12h/l`, `SP q` 0/4/5/6, the replies of `5n`, `6n`, `c`, `>c`, and `14t`, and the explicit error of the unsupported `18t`. `implemented OSC sequences change the title, colors, cursor, and clipboard and reply` covers OSC 0, 2, 4 with its query, 104 with and without a number, 10–12 with queries and 110–112, 50, and 52 denied by default and allowed through `terminal.clipboard.program`. Both passed three runs per host on rebuilt Wails and Tauri. The check found V5-51-6-1 and V5-51-6-2, and showed that the inventory's modes are not all implemented: ?1 (cursor keys), ?2004, ?1007, and the wheel report of the mouse modes work, while button and motion reports, focus reports, and the application keypad do not (V5-51-6-3 to V5-51-6-5). Vendor OSC 7, 8, 9, 133, and 1337 have their own window checks (V5-51-1 to V5-51-4 and the inline image check). V5-51-6-3 and V5-51-6-4 are done; the window check `mouse, focus, paste, and alternate-scroll modes report input` also reads ?1 through alternate scrolling (`ESC O A`/`B` with ?1, `ESC [ A`/`B` without) on both hosts. Remaining: the real-input keypad check of V5-51-6-5. Done on 2026-09-25: V5-51-6-1 to V5-51-6-5 are done, and every implemented CSI and OSC sequence has a window or real-input check that passed on both hosts.
      - [o] V5-51-6-1 — P1: Keep the page's cursor state equal to the drawn cursor. On 2026-09-25 the protocol check found on Tauri that after `terminal.focus` the region was the native first responder while `terminal.cursor.focused` stayed false until the next output, and `terminal.cursor.shape` reported the configured policy instead of the shape a program requested with DECSCUSR. Acceptance: the measured cause, a fix, contract and plugin tests, and the window check on both hosts. Done on 2026-09-25. Cause: `present_screen` sent only the raster; the callers that changed the screen through output, scrolling, or input also sent the screen event, but focus, theme, cursor-policy, selection, and configuration redraws did not, so the page kept the previous cursor. A `screen.read` request showed the sidecar already focused. The page also copied only the position, visibility, and focus from the screen cursor. Fix: `present_screen` sends the screen event of every raster it presents, the callers send a screen event themselves only when no image region exists, and `terminal.cursor` reports `drawn` (shape and blink request after the program's request and the focus rule). Red: the contract test `a_focus_change_sends_the_screen_with_the_presentation_that_draws_it` (an idle sidecar answered the focus change without a screen event) and the plugin test `the drawn cursor follows the screen event while the policy shape stays` failed before the change. Green: vt-core tests (67 contract tests), the terminal plugin tests 104 of 104, the VT recovery test, and on rebuilt Wails and Tauri the CSI protocol window check passed three runs per host; a full window run passed 167 of 171 (Tauri drag checks refused while the application was active, recorded in V5-63-1, a Wails drag delay of 54.7 ms at a load average of 18.5, recorded in F8-6-2, and the inline image failure of V5-53), and the real-input tier passed 37 of 38 with the long-history wheel check of V5-60 measuring a 64 ms stall at a load average of about 24.
      - [o] V5-51-6-2 — P2: Report the text area of `CSI 14t` in the unit of inline image pixels. On 2026-09-25 the protocol check read `ESC[4;288;976t` for an 18×122 terminal with 15.5×8-point cells on a scale-2 display: the sidecar rounded the cell size to points (16×8), so the height was not the text area, while inline image pixel sizes are device pixels. Acceptance: a fix and the window check on both hosts. Done on 2026-09-25. The sidecar passes the device-pixel cell size (31×16) to the engine, so `14t` reports `ESC[4;558;1952t`, and a program that derives the cell size from it requests inline images that fit the cells. Red: the CSI protocol window check failed on the reply above. Green on rebuilt Wails and Tauri: the check passed three runs per host, and the engine and vt-core tests pass.
      - [o] V5-51-6-3 — P1: Report mouse buttons and motion to programs. The sidecar encodes only wheel reports; a press, release, or drag in a terminal whose program enabled ?1000, ?1002, or ?1003 selects text instead of reporting it, and ?1005 and ?1006 apply only to the wheel. Acceptance: presses, releases, drags, and (for ?1003) moves are reported in the default, UTF-8, and SGR encodings with modifier bits, Shift keeps selecting, and window checks read the reports on both hosts. Done on 2026-09-25. The page sends each left-button press, movement while pressed, release, and each movement without a button into another cell as a `mouse` operation; the sidecar tracks ?1000, ?1002, and ?1003 separately and, for a press without Shift, reports the press, the release, and the movements that the mode requires in the default, UTF-8, or SGR encoding with the Alt and Control bits, while the selection operations of that gesture select nothing. The unused SGR-only `encode_mouse` and `encode_scroll` were replaced by one encoder that the wheel report also uses. Tests: the encoder unit tests (SGR release keeps the button, the default and UTF-8 encodings release as button 3, modifier bits, the default encoding's column limit, a two-byte UTF-8 coordinate), the contract tests `a_drag_is_reported_as_press_motion_and_release_when_the_program_tracks_button_motion`, `a_click_program_receives_press_and_release_but_no_motion`, `a_shift_drag_selects_instead_of_reporting`, `pointer_input_is_not_reported_without_a_mouse_mode`, `any_motion_tracking_reports_moves_without_a_button_once_per_cell`, and `an_invalid_mouse_operation_is_rejected`, the updated plugin tests, the window check `mouse, paste, and alternate-scroll modes report input` (?1000/?1006 click, ?1005 at column 100, ?1002 drag, wheel reports, ?1007, ?2004) and the real-input check `a real pointer move is reported with ?1003 and a real Shift drag selects while ?1002 is on`, which passed on rebuilt Wails and Tauri. An attempt to show the window check failing on the previous sidecar was invalid, because the check applications run the service from the root `target/debug`, which the attempt did not rebuild; the red evidence is the absence of a `mouse` operation in the previous sidecar. The full window and real-input runs are pending for the final batch.
