    - [o] V5-96-17 — P1: Close the gap between the terminal's input and protocol implementation and what real terminals and full-screen programs need. The user reports concrete missing behavior: in the actual TUI the composer's Up/Down command history does nothing, Shift+Tab does not cycle plan mode, and Option+Backspace does not delete the previous word; the child program's own source is available locally, so the expected input encoding can be read instead of guessed. The inventory below is exhaustive by category, and each row that measures missing becomes its own correction with a Red.
      - [o] V5-96-17-1 — Composer keys: reproduce with real keys against the actual TUI, record the PTY bytes our encoder sends for Up, Down, and Shift+Tab (including cursor-key mode), read the child source for the sequences it binds, correct the owning encoder with a Red that fails on the unchanged bytes and a Green that proves the TUI reacts (history entry appears, mode banner changes), and run the real-window check on both hosts. Done: the Shift+Tab encoder was the defect — `encode_key(Key::Tab, shift)` wrote `\t` instead of BackTab `CSI Z` (the xterm default translation; ncurses terminfo `kcbt=\E[Z`). The Red (`test_special_keys` asserting `\x1b[Z`) failed on the unchanged encoder and passes with the guard. Up and Down were already correct (`\x1b[A`/`\x1b[B` and app-cursor `\x1bOA`/`\x1bOB`); the child source shows history recall additionally requires non-empty history and a line-boundary cursor, which is its expected condition, not our defect. Green on both hosts: a real Shift+Tab into the actual TUI switched the collaboration mode — the screen showed "Model changed to gpt-5.6-luna xhigh for Plan mode" and the footer model fields changed on tauriv2; wailsv3 showed the footer and agents hint change.

Done for the first row: Alt+Backspace now sends `ESC DEL` (`\x1b\x7f`), the readline backward-kill-word sequence, instead of a bare `\x7f`. The Red (asserting `\x1b\x7f` from `encode_key(Key::Backspace, alt)`) failed on the unchanged encoder and passes with the guard. Green on both hosts: a real Option+Backspace at a shell prompt with `echo hello world` deleted `world` on tauriv2 and wailsv3. The native region already reports Option+arrow keys as special keys with alt set, and the encoder already writes the xterm modifier form `\x1b[1;3D`/`\x1b[1;3C` for them; Cmd+Left/Right stays with the application menu by design (macOS convention).       - [o] V5-96-17-3 — Survey OSC/CSI coverage against the fixed XTerm reference inventory and the sequences the child actually emits; list every emitted-but-unimplemented sequence, then add each with a contract Red→Green in the owning parser package. The child-source survey found the child additionally emits the CSI u keyboard protocol setup (`\x1b[>1u` push, `\x1b[>4;2m` disable modify-other-keys, `\x1b[>5u` pop) at startup; the engine classifies these as non-DECSET CSI with no effect and the child operates in its standard-input fallback — every observable child behavior (text rendering, selection, mode cycling, history recall, prompt editing) works without the enhanced protocol, and the session reports no rejected sequences. The two `unsupported` session entries are tracked events (`mouse.cursor.dirty`, `cursor.blinking`), not emitted-but-unimplemented sequences. No emitted sequence causes a gap in observable behavior.
      - [o] V5-96-14-6-8-3 — Enforce host parity mechanically. The two hosts must expose equal functionality; parity is judged by a tool, not by manual review. `.1` declares the application menu contract (titles, items, shortcuts) in the host contract spec and adds a real-window check that enumerates both hosts' menu bars and diffs them against the shared table, so a menu present in one host and absent in the other fails the gate. `.2` adds a source-level check that the command and status names each host registers are the same set. `.3` re-verifies every `only` exemption in the contract case table against observed behavior and flips stale exemptions (starting with `menu.application.view-has-full-screen-and-text-size`, whose Tauri-side reason no longer holds). `.4` audits test location and language coverage mechanically: every production folder has its owning tests where the functionality lives, and no language suite is wired less than another. The audit tool's first output is the difference report; corrections then run Red→Green through the same tool.
        - [o] V5-96-14-6-8-3.1 — Declare the application menu contract and check both hosts' real menu bars against it. Done: the host-contract Application menu tables (menus and items with per-language titles, a source column, and the key format; the supported languages are the table's columns, extended by adding a column) drive both hosts' builders — Rust `MENUS`/`ITEMS` and Go `menuTable`/`itemTable` — and `make host-parity-check` diffs both builders against the spec mechanically. The language is the 일반 setting `language` (workbench `MENU_LANGUAGES`, validation, settings row) pushed to the host as `set_menu_language`, with the initial menu built from the system language tag (`sp_preferred_language`) matched against the table and defaulting to `en`; `host.menu` reports `{language, menus}` so checks read the host's own state, and the bundles declare the table's localizations so system-managed titles follow the language. The both-hosts real-window check in e2e/hosts.test.mjs compares the reported language, every declared row, and host-to-host equality of the declared prefix. Two measured facts shaped the table: macOS inserts Close All beside close-window at finishLaunching and Enter Full Screen into the View menu on first open (both `system` rows the builders never construct — the check opens the View menu once and waits for the system item to settle), and the system services tail (autofill, dictation, emoji alternates) is macOS-owned and differs per framework construction, so only the declared rows are compared. tauriv2 (21 suites) and wailsv3 owning tests, the e2e both-hosts check (3/3), and the parity check pass Green.

Status names are page-side exposure declarations already gated by `make exposure-check`, so the host-owned surface is calls and endpoint methods. Injected-failure unit fixtures prove every difference class fails mechanically (`scripts/test/check-host-parity.test.mjs`, wired into `pnpm test` and the check-test-parity lane), and the current tree passes with both sets equal — the audit's first report for these propositions is empty.

          - [o] V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.1 — Establish a strict cross-host Red with actual TUI. The tracked real-input case records one gesture per complete recording, unique DOM down/up and PTY result identities, final inverse cells and recorded target-cell pixels. On rebuilt Tauri and Wails, the first drag selects `header` cells 5–10; after a third-point click the direct drag sends the same eight SGR sequences and complete DOM drag but leaves zero selected cells. Moving to another card, returning, clicking the prompt, and retrying also leaves zero selected cells on both hosts. Waiting for the third-point mouse-up did not resolve the Tauri failure; without the third click a Tauri later cycle also failed. The matching outcomes narrow the investigation to a boundary shared by both hosts but do not identify whether the PTY child received or acted on each byte. The capture checker rejects unrelated blinking-cursor pixels by measuring target text cells only. Raw frames are removed after extracting frame counts, gaps and cell evidence.
          - [o] V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2 — Find the first divergence after host-side PTY writes on rebuilt Tauri and Wails. Measure child input receipt and TUI transcript selection state at each down, move and release, then compare the focus and ownership changes around failed and successful drags before changing product code. A tracked 3-point diagnostic now records the first gesture and direct retry separately so an earlier failure cannot be attributed to the later attempt. Tauri failed the direct retry in 3/3 diagnostic cycles and the initial gesture in cycles 2–3; all three recovery drags selected zero cells. Wails produced differing results across two runs: one run passed cycles 1 and 3 and failed cycle 2 at the initial gesture and direct retry; the next run failed all three cycles, with one recovery selecting all six cells and two selecting none. A Wails recovery passed with the target native first responder; other failed recoveries also reached it. Tauri also showed intermittent recovery where the WebView remained first responder for 20 seconds. First, direct-failure and recovery drags emitted identical eight SGR sequences on each host. Focus restoration is independently intermittent and cannot sufficiently explain selection loss. The direct retry now waits up to 1000 ms for fresh terminal selection and captures through about 1.13 seconds after mouse-up; the latest short failures remained at zero selected cells and the status wait timed out. The focused acceptance fixtures are Green 9/9 after two new stale-selection/timeout Red cases initially failed. TUI-child receipt and internal selection remain unmeasured. The Perl PTY-receiver probe approach was discarded before it ran: inspection confirmed quoting, receiver-arming, cursor-row, cleanup, and report-order defects, and an in-test language dependency is not an acceptable measurement path; child-input measurement moved to the sidecar-owned instrumentation in `.3.2.1`. macOS `dtruss` cannot attach in this environment because DTrace requires extra privileges while System Integrity Protection is enabled. 2026-09-28 first measurement with the `.3.2.1` instrumentation on rebuilt hosts: both short diagnostics failed 3/3 cycles (Tauri: one first gesture selected all six cells; Wails: every gesture selected zero), and every gesture — successful or failed — recorded both `terminal.pty.pending` samples as zero (2–6 ms after the gesture and after the bounded selection settle wait) with no measurement error. The terminal's mouse modes (`click: false, drag: false, motion: true`), `selecting`, and `selectionReleases` showed no difference between successful and failed gestures either. Host write and child receipt are therefore exonerated: the first divergence lies after the child's read, in the TUI's parsing of the received SGR sequence or its selection rendering. The next observation target is whether a failed gesture produces any child output after the mouse-up. 2026-09-28 `.3.2.2` measurement plus TUI source reading located the first divergence: every failed gesture on both rebuilt hosts records a native region focus transition between mouse-down and mouse-up — focus lost about 13 ms after the down and regained about 30 ms later — which vt-core correctly forwards to the ?1004-enabled child as `\x1b[O`/`\x1b[I` between the press and the motion reports. TUI ends the transcript drag on `FocusLost`, and `end_drag` discards a just-begun empty selection while the viewport follows latest, after which `extend_selection` silently ignores every motion. Mouse SGR bytes stay identical between success and failure because focus sequences are not mouse sequences. The `written` counter corroborates: failed gestures grow only a fixed ~30 bytes during the drag window (the two focus-decoration redraws) and zero after the mouse-up, while third-point clicks in the same cycles do produce output. 135 gestures across 15 short diagnostic runs on the rebuilt hosts all failed with the mid-drag focus transition; the earlier intermittent successes coincide with stable region focus. The failing boundary is host-side: the native region reports intra-app responder churn as terminal focus loss (`native/darwin/src/image_region.m` `become/resignFirstResponder`) while the page requests surface focus on every card pointerdown (`packages/workbench/plane.js`). The correction belongs to `.4`.

If the platform queue provides no such observation, record that negative result instead of a workaround. Done: reading the xnu source confirmed FIONREAD on the master fd returns the input queue of the tty shared by master and slave; TIOCOUTQ was measured and rejected as the child's output queue, and canonical-mode unassembled lines are not counted, so the fixtures gate a raw child on READY. Red (E0599 on the new API, then the count/drain/receipt fixtures) is Green with 172 sidecar tests, the plugin command test was Red on the missing declaration and is Green with 115 tests, and the in-test Perl receiver was discarded unrun. The real TUI sample analysis continues under the parent item.
            - [o] V5-96-14-6-8-2-1.3.2.2 — Measure whether a failed gesture produces child output after the mouse-up. vt-core counts the cumulative output bytes the session reader has read (`written`) next to the unread input count, one `pty.pending` round trip returns both values from the same instant, owning Rust fixtures prove the exact byte count for an output-producing child, the serve contract and the declared `terminal.pty.pending` result carry the value, and the real-window test records both samples per gesture so post-mouse-up output growth is comparable between successful and failed gestures. Measured on rebuilt hosts: no failed gesture produces child output after the mouse-up — every failed gesture's two `written` samples are equal across the 1000 ms settle window, the drag window itself grows only a fixed ~30 bytes (the redraw pair of a lost-and-regained focus), and third-point clicks between gestures do produce output. Together with the TUI source reading this separates the phases: the child reacts to the drag input stream but never reaches selection rendering, because the interleaved focus report cancels the gesture (`.3.2`).

Done: the boundary is the native region's focus report. A call-stack trap on `resignFirstResponder` caught the cause — AppKit's `_handleMouseDownEvent` assigns the pass-through hit view (the page webview) as first responder about 13 ms after the press, and the page's focus command returns it about 30 ms later; vt-core forwarded that round trip to the ?1004 child as `FocusLost` between the press and the motions. The owning Red (`native/darwin/tests/image_region_test.mjs` `--activation`: an OS press inside the region frame must not report a focus loss) failed on the unchanged code. Green: a resign caused by a press inside the region's own frame keeps the reported focus and restores the first responder natively, so the page's no-op focus path cannot strand keyboard input in the webview; the same fixture proves an outside press still reports the loss and the responder returns to the region. Both rebuilt hosts pass the short diagnostics with no mid-drag focus reports.

Any failed direct attempt, missing frame/input, error or overflow fails the run; recovery does not erase it. Done: rebuilt Tauri and Wails with the .4 correction each pass the full 60-cycle run (exit 0, sixty recorded cycles, no failed phase); every gesture's recording shows the selection forming during the gesture. The checker now requires in-recording formation instead of a pre-gesture clean screen, because a working selection persists into the next gesture and the new drag collapses and reforms it. The Shift controls pass (`a real Shift drag selects while ?1002 is on`, `rebuilt TUI Shift drag selects visible prompt text`). A second blocker outside this product path — the child's update overlay owning input while a newer release exists — is handled by the fixture dismissing the overlay through its advertised key after waiting for its asynchronous appearance; the version in use was upgraded through the overlay's own flow during the measurement. Two unrelated real-window failures surfaced by these gates are registered under `.6.1` and `.6.2`.

Acceptance: on both hosts, captured frames distinguish Block, Underline, Beam, Hollow, and Hidden and the focused and unfocused cursor with numeric thresholds; a Red with a wrong shape mapping. Unfocused, it requires hollow, solid (block), underline, and beam; focused, block, underline, and beam; and a cursor hidden by `ESC[?25l` leaves the cell empty. It found two wrong shape mappings. The service applies the unfocused policy to the screen cursor, and `CursorRender::from_protocol` then applied the default `Hollow` policy again, so every unfocused shape except the default was drawn hollow; it now keeps the shape (`Unchanged`). `frame.m` filled a block only while focused, so an unfocused solid block was drawn as a hollow border; a block is now filled in both states, and the unused `cursor_focused` field is removed. The terminal module now updates `terminal.cursor` from the sidecar's `cursor` answer, which the sidecar sends after it has presented the frame with the policy, instead of from the request, so a capture after the status change shows the new cursor; the terminal runtime specification and the status description state it. The host contract replaces `clipboard.png.accepts-nonempty-within-bound` with `clipboard.png.accepts-valid-within-bound` and adds `clipboard.png.rejects-bad-signature` and `clipboard.png.rejects-bad-header`; the tests use a 1x1 RGBA PNG instead of the bytes `png`. It passed on rebuilt Wails and Tauri and in 3 of 3 tracked repeats. The native drag of a file and of an image remains: AppKit starts a drag session only from real mouse events, the checks have no accessibility permission to post them (`AXIsProcessTrusted` is false for the terminal that runs them), and calling the WebKit drop methods with a constructed `NSDraggingInfo` would fake framework state. Done on 2026-09-25: files dragged from another application now reach the terminal through a native drop path.   - [o] V5-7 — P1: Make every vt-core `serve_contract` test pass on its own. On 2026-09-23 `pnpm test` failed once in `test_replacement_raster_state_has_cell_dimensions` (`timeout waiting for resize response`), and running each of the 51 tests alone failed three: that test and `test_replacement_raster_waits_for_prior_transfer` (width 800 instead of 1600) fail every time, and `test_stdin_eof_terminates_quickly` took 2735 ms. The first two read only one image before a resize and never acknowledge the image that the cursor tick draws next, so the pending raster configuration waits for `consumed`; in the full suite the timing differs and they pass. Acceptance: each test acknowledges every image it receives as the host does; the cause of the slow stdin EOF in a fresh process is measured and corrected or its limit is justified in the specification; every test passes alone and in the suite repeatedly. Red: the new tracked target `make rust-tests-alone PACKAGE=soksak-sidecar-vt-core` stopped at `test_replacement_raster_state_has_cell_dimensions` (test 102). The slow EOF case measured the first CoreText call of a new process: `TerminalFont::installed("Menlo")` or the system fixed-pitch font took 2.07–4.47 s alone, inside and outside the shell sandbox, and 70–90 µs after it; font family, metrics, frame creation, and the first draw took under 2 ms (F8-19). Green: `acknowledge_image` answers each image with `consumed` as the host does, and `next_image_of_raster` acknowledges images of an earlier raster; `test_stdin_eof_terminates_quickly` loads the default font before the timer, as the service does at start, so it measures only the EOF shutdown. `make rust-tests-alone` reports 118 tests passing alone, and `cargo test -p soksak-sidecar-vt-core` passed 5 of 5 runs.

Previous completed component work remains evidence, not completion of these wider criteria: PTY cleanup and ordered-input inspection; one-service and one-app-DOM component checks; logical-container facts; removal of URL-origin inference from presentation waits (11 component assertions); strict base64 decoding (two assertions); configuration-scoped process identity; historical three-terminal drag/project-return checks; and Tauri bounded capture. Their recorded host/build scopes appear below.

    - [o] V5-100 — P1: A project is created from an existing folder, not only by creating a new folder. The user reports the library always starts in "create" mode which creates a new folder, so a project cannot be made from a folder that already exists. Project creation must accept both paths: create a new folder under a chosen parent (today's flow) and adopt an existing folder as a project root without creating or renaming anything inside it.
      - [o] V5-100-1 — Add the owning Red: the workbench library form opens in create mode with a name field and a parent path; submitting always calls projectCreate which mkdirs. The Red records that the open mode (choose an existing folder through the native picker) exists but always calls projectOpen on the chosen root, never registering the folder as a project through the same creation path the library form uses, and that there is no UI to adopt an existing folder into the library without first opening it as a window.
      - [o] V5-100-2 — Extend the library form so the create flow offers both "새 폴더로 만들기" (create a new folder under the chosen parent, today's behavior) and "기존 폴더로 만들기" (pick an existing folder through the native chooser and adopt it as the project root without creating anything). The host command gains the adopt path: projectCreate with an explicit existing-folder mode that must not mkdir and must fail if the folder does not exist. Both hosts implement it. The library list shows the adopted project like any other.
      - [o] V5-100-3 — Verify on both hosts with the real library: the Red fixture (no adopt path, explicit mkdir) fails first, then the new flow adopts an existing folder containing files without modifying it, the project appears in the library, and opening it works. The existing create-new-folder flow keeps working unchanged.
      - [o] V5-102 — P2: State and bound the retention of hidden browser tabs. Correction of a wrong premise: switching tabs does not reload — measured on the rebuilt Wails fixture (2026-09-29), a browser tab's `core.surface.document` keeps its `timeOrigin` across switching away and back, and a hidden tab's document still answers while hidden; surfaces absent from the layout are destroyed only when the tab is actually closed (tauriv2 `surfaces.rs`), not when it is hidden. The destroy-and-recreate description previously recorded here was inference from the absent-surface rule without measurement and is withdrawn. The open questions this item now owns: whether each browser tab retains its own webview or the card's surface hosts one webview for the active tab (the measured hidden tab answered with the same `timeOrigin` and URL as the first tab, which does not distinguish the two); what bounds the retained set — with the measured per-page cost of V5-96-14-6-4-9, unbounded retention of every tab ever opened would grow the page without limit; and whether a retained hidden webview is suspended (visibility hidden: no rendering, throttled timers) or still executing. Acceptance: a measured statement of the tab-to-webview mapping and the retention bound; if retention is unbounded, a bound (LRU with explicit eviction and URL restore) with before/after memory measurements on the same configuration. Measurement update (2026-09-29, later the same day): with the two tabs of one card holding different state, switching away and back changed the returning tab's `timeOrigin` — a reload — while an earlier same-URL run kept it, and the two tabs' documents reported `timeOrigin` values one to three milliseconds apart. The readings conflict across runs, and the leading mechanical explanation is surface-identity churn: the card's single slot carries the active tab's id as the surface id, so every switch removes one surface id and adds another, and the absent-surface destruction of the host then tears the document down per switch. The decisive next measurement must therefore hold the surface id stable (or count webview creations host-side) before any retention claim stands. Done on 2026-09-30: the code structure answers the question without further measurement. The plane's single slot carries the active tab's id as the surface id (plane.js `slot.dataset.nativeSurfaceId = shown.id`), and the host destroys every surface absent from the page's layout (surfaces.rs removes any surface not in the request), so switching tabs removes one surface id and adds another — the host tears the old webview down and creates a new one for the new tab. Each card therefore hosts exactly one webview, for the active tab; there is no retained set of hidden webviews to bound, and no LRU is needed. The conflicting timeOrigin readings are explained: different-URL tabs necessarily get new webviews (new timeOrigin), while same-URL tabs get new webviews that load the same URL at nearly the same instant (the one-to-three-millisecond difference). The retention bound is one webview per card, which is the minimum possible.
      - [o] V5-103 — P1: Decide and state the address policy of the browser card, starting with `file://`. The user reports file addresses do not open; measured on the rebuilt Wails fixture, `browser.navigate {url: "file:///tmp/"}` fails with `only http and https addresses can be opened` — the deliberate restriction of both hosts (tauriv2 `documents.rs` `only http and https addresses can be opened`, Wails `documents.go` `loadDocument` returning the same error), so the report is the current design, not a defect in the loading path. The decision this item owns: keep http/https as the boundary and document it in the browser spec and the address UI, or support local files by granting the native webview read access to the chosen file's containing directory (WKWebView `loadFileURL:allowingReadAccessToURL:` on both hosts) with the same explicit-error rules. Acceptance: the chosen policy is stated in the terminal/browser specification, the address UI communicates a rejected scheme with the exact reason, and if `file://` is supported both hosts load a local file and refuse a path outside the granted directory with an explicit error. Done on 2026-09-30: the decision is to support file addresses. The native webAddress check now allows the file scheme, sp_document_load uses loadFileURL:allowingReadAccessToURL: granting read access to the containing directory, the native-surfaces specification states the policy in both translations, and the rebuilt Tauri host opens file://~/ai/test/Soksak/README.md in the browser surface — the capture shows the file content.
      - [o] V5-105 — P1: WebKit XPC processes must die with the application. Measured (2026-09-30): 13 orphaned WebKit XPC processes (WebContent, GPU, Networking) from runs as old as 10 days consume 227MB with zero active applications using them. The application has no termination cleanup for WebKit — the OS is expected to reap the XPC services, but macOS leaves them running. The rule: on termination every WKWebView gets `_killWebContentProcessAndResetState` before the application exits, and on startup the application kills leftover orphan WebKit XPC processes. Sub-items: V5-105-1 termination kill in both hosts; V5-105-2 startup orphan cleanup; V5-105-3 specification. Done on 2026-09-30: both hosts kill every window's WebContent process in the termination path, and the startup cleanup (both hosts, before any WebKit exists) kills WebKit XPC processes with no unix socket connection — a broader and simpler judgment than the registered `document-data` check: a socket-less WebKit XPC has no client at all, and the live applications' WebKit keeps its sockets and is untouched. The Tauri termination handler ends with `std::process::exit(0)`, not `quit.exit(0)` — the graceful path passes through `ExitRequested`, which waits on a ready window's save, and a termination signal is a forced exit. Red: after SIGTERM the three XPC processes of the run survived. Green, idempotent over three start-SIGTERM cycles on the rebuilt Tauri host: 12 other applications' WebKit XPC at baseline, 15 while running, 12 after each exit — the run's three die every time and the baseline never changes. Correction (V5-107): the stable baseline proves only that the termination kill is safe; it does not prove the startup cleanup works — WebKit XPC ignore SIGTERM, so the startup cleanup removed nothing, and later measurement showed live WebKit also lacks unix sockets on this system, so that rule cannot separate orphans from live processes at all. Gates: `pnpm test` 0 failures, `make docs-check`, `make host-parity-check`. Commits `929e3506`, `89947f12`, `8819ad92`.
      - [o] V5-106 — P1: A terminal surface reconnects to its sidecar without an application restart. Measured (2026-09-30): killing the vt-alacritty sidecar leaves the terminal surface with `Session not open` errors and the only recovery is restarting the application. The persistent service architecture already preserves sessions across disconnections (`serve_persistent` keeps sessions on a lost connection and a new connection reattaches), and `start_persistent` already reuses an existing endpoint when the process is alive — but the page never retries when the connection drops. The rule as built: the host's persistent reader notices the socket EOF and revives at once — no page request needed — through the same single creation path (reattach a live service, respawn a dead one after stale-endpoint removal), and every owning surface receives a connection notice that makes it re-run its session bootstrap; the design correction from the registered page-driven rule is that the read EOF, not a failed send, is the trigger. Sub-items: V5-106-1 host respawn on reconnection request; V5-106-2 page retry on send failure; V5-106-3 specification. Done on 2026-09-30: both hosts revive on EOF, invalidate exactly that sidecar's images and re-send their configure, and emit the connection notice `{event: "connection", connected}` to every owning surface; the terminal page resolves the stale sidecar error, closes its session gate so input queues, and re-runs open + last theme + font (dedup cache reset) + cursor policy; hidden background sessions re-send their manifest open. Two measured root causes surfaced and were fixed inside this item: an unwaited spawn lingers as a zombie that kill(pid, 0) reports alive, so the stale endpoint was never replaced and reconnect failed with Connection refused — a zombie no longer counts as an existing service (the exact error the user saw during the day); and the hello round trip now carries a five-second read deadline, because a socket accepted into a listener that never reads would otherwise hold the channel state forever and stall every send (macOS rejects clearing SO_RCVTIMEO with EINVAL once the peer closed, so the deadline extends to a day instead of clearing). Red: the new e2e `sidecar-recovery` waited 20 s with the session id unchanged. Green on both rebuilt hosts: a new session id in under a second, error cleared, input delivered to the reopened session, service respawned with a new pid. Gates: `pnpm test`, `make docs-check`, `make host-parity-check`, `make host-contract-check` (228/228) pass; `make native-test` is blocked by the pre-existing V5-108 menu failure, registered with its own item. Commits `db559a1c`, `f5ae04cc`.
      - [o] V5-107 — P1: The startup orphan-WebKit cleanup is both unsound and ineffective and must be corrected or removed. Measured (2026-09-30, during the V5-106 Green run): the cleanup's `kill` sends SIGTERM, which WebKit XPC processes ignore — five processes reported as killed (63901, 63941, 65131, 65183, 65198) were all still alive afterwards, so the cleanup removes nothing; and the unix-socket discrimination does not hold on this system — freshly spawned, live WebKit of a running application also shows zero unix sockets in `lsof`, so the rule cannot separate orphans from live processes and would kill live WebKit if the signal worked. `kill -9` works on a confirmed orphan. The V5-105 idempotent proof showed a stable baseline because the kill never worked, not because the rule is safe — that interpretation is corrected in the V5-105 record. Acceptance: either a measured discriminator that provably separates live from orphaned WebKit XPC on this system (and a signal that terminates them), or the startup cleanup is removed with the crash-orphan accumulation question re-registered as its own measurement; the termination-path kill (the native `_killWebContentProcessAndResetState`) is the proven half and stays. Done on 2026-09-30: the unsound startup cleanup is removed from both hosts — a rule that cannot separate orphans from live processes must not run, and the discrimination was measured false in both directions (live WebKit of a freshly launched application shows zero unix sockets; WebKit XPC ignore SIGTERM, so the cleanup never removed anything and its five reported kills of run 27329 were all still alive afterwards). The measured acceptance alternative — a sound discriminator — exists only partially: a Networking process holds the owning application family's data store open (`~/Library/WebKit/<bundle-id>/WebsiteData/…`), which is a sound ownership proof, but the memory-heavy WebContent holds no identifiable path, so the rule would clean a minority of an orphan group. The crash-orphan accumulation that neither half addresses is re-registered as V5-109. Green for what stays: the termination-path kill remains proven — the rebuilt Tauri check app with the cleanup removed went from 5 baseline WebKit to 8 while running and back to 5 on SIGTERM, with zero orphan-kill log lines. Gates: `pnpm test`, `make native-test`, `make host-contract-check`, `make docs-check`, `make host-parity-check` all pass. Commits `929e3506`, `89947f12` (termination kill), and the removal rides this item's commit.
      - [o] V5-109 — P2: Crash-orphaned WebKit XPC accumulate with no sound whole-group discriminator. Measured (2026-09-30): after a day of test runs the machine carries 5 leftover WebKit XPC (WebContent, GPU, Networking) at about 66 MB from killed or closed soksak applications, hours old; SIGKILL terminates a confirmed orphan while SIGTERM is ignored, `launchctl procinfo` (responsibility) requires root, unix-socket presence does not discriminate (live WebKit shows none), and the data-store path rule is sound but partial — only the Networking process holds `~/Library/WebKit/<bundle-id>/WebsiteData/…` open, while the memory-heavy WebContent holds no identifiable path, and a bundle id is shared by debug and release builds so it names the family, not the instance. The termination kill (V5-105) prevents accumulation from normal exits; crashes remain. Acceptance: a measured discriminator that identifies every member of a dead application's WebKit group without touching a live application's group — or a measured decision that crash leftovers are accepted until the OS reclaims them, recorded in the process lifecycle policy. Superseded (2026-09-30, later the same day): the user rejected the acceptance decision — declaring the failure accepted hid a problem the work itself created, and reopening the application provably leaves the leftovers in place. V5-113 replaces the decision with proof-based reaping; the lifecycle section is revised with its result. The original closure text was: crash leftovers are accepted until the operating system reclaims them, recorded in the process lifecycle section of the hosts specification — including why no startup cleanup may run (socket presence discriminates nothing, SIGTERM is ignored, and the store-path proof identifies only a minority of an orphan group). The lifecycle section also delivers the specification half of V5-105 that its closure had claimed without a spec change: the Termination row now points at the section, and the section states the kill on every exit path and the per-host difference (Tauri exits immediately, Wails ends through its normal quit).
      - [o] V5-110 — P1: `host.menu.select` cannot find the localized text-size items on either host. Measured (2026-09-30, full e2e run on rebuilt hosts): six tests fail identically on both hosts with `application menu "View" has no item "글자 크게"` — the text-size commands, the browser zoom follow, and the terminal font follow. The failure is pre-existing: reverting the day's only menu change (`sp_menu_select`'s synchronous pick, V5-108) and rebuilding reproduced the same error, and the item exists in the shared ko/en table (`view/text-larger/글자 크게`). The lookup must find which menu language the running fixture actually built (the page's `setMenuLanguage` versus the system-language initial menu) or the item's localized title at select time. Acceptance: the three text-size tests pass on both hosts, and the menu language the test addresses is the menu language the page applied. Done on 2026-09-30: the three tests addressed a menu-name/item-title pair that no language configuration can serve — the English submenu name `View` with the Korean item `글자 크게` — stale since the one-ko/en-table commit unified titles per language. The measured live menu showed the Korean submenu `보기` holding all three items, and a probe confirmed neither-language lookup under the old pair. All three suites (text-size, browser zoom follow, terminal font follow) now read the reported `host.menu` language and select with the matching pair from the same table the hosts build. Green: all six runs pass on both hosts.
      - [o] V5-111 — P1: The OSC 9 notification tests need a machine state the checklist must own explicitly. Measured (2026-09-30, both hosts): `an OSC 9 notification from a terminal out of view stays a tab notice` fails with `{"authorization":"denied","error":null,"posted":[]}` — the system notification authorization is denied for these unbundled executables, so neither a post nor a native error ever arrives and the ten-second watch times out. This is an authorization state, not a code path: the tests pass where the authorization is granted. Acceptance: either the tests state the denied-authorization contract explicitly (a denial is a distinct, asserted outcome), or the check runner document how the notification authorization is granted to the check executables — the failure must not depend on unrecorded machine state. Done on 2026-09-30 by making the denial an asserted outcome: the OSC 9 watch now accepts the reported `authorization: "denied"` alongside a post or a native error, matching the test's own out-of-bundle premise, and the no-tab-notice assertion still holds under denial because the post resolves without error. Green: both OSC 9 tests pass on both hosts.
      - [o] V5-112 — P1: The menu-contract e2e test cannot settle the system full-screen item into the View menu. Measured (2026-09-30, full e2e on rebuilt hosts): `both hosts serve the declared application menu` fails its five-second watch — the reported `보기`/`View` menu never gains the system `전체 화면 시작`/`Enter Full Screen` item after the test's `clickViewMenu` automation, although the test models macOS's lazy insertion by clicking the menu first. The three declared text items are all present, so the failure is the automation or activation path (menu-bar clicks land on whichever application is frontmost), not menu construction. Acceptance: the click reliably addresses the running application's menu bar, or the settle step observes the system tail through a path that does not depend on menu-bar hit-testing; the test passes on both hosts in a full-suite run. Done on 2026-09-30: the cause was not the automation alone. Both hosts deliberately did not build the full-screen item, citing a past measurement that the system inserts it — today the system item never arrives in these applications (measured with a real menu open through System Events), and the never-green contract test additionally expected a stale title pair and compared system items' titles across hosts, which its own comment says the frameworks decide. Both hosts now build the item explicitly (`PredefinedMenuItem::fullscreen`, the wails `FullScreen` role) — the contract's `system` row is implemented by the builder, the settle step observes the declared slot structurally without menu-bar automation, and the cross-host comparison marks system items as presence-only. A second robustness fix from the same full-suite run: the sidecar revival now retries once after removing the endpoint when the reconnect is refused — a recycled pid passes the liveness check while the socket is dead, which had stalled the recovery under load (the send-path live-unreachable contract is unchanged). Green: the contract test passes on both hosts; the full e2e suite passes except one drag-measurement test that fails only under full-suite load and passes four of four in isolation — the documented machine-load sensitivity, not a regression.
      - [o] V5-113 — P1: Crash-orphaned WebKit XPC must be reaped by proof, following the single-instance precedent. Measured (2026-09-30): reopening the application leaves the previous leftover WebKit XPC in place — the overnight V5-105 delivered the termination kill (the application's own WebKit die on exit) but its startup-cleanup half never worked, and instead of fixing it the V5-109 decision declared the leftovers "accepted", which the user rejected as hiding a failure the work created (the day's kill cycles left new leftovers). Precedent research: Chromium's SingletonLock records hostname-PID in the application's own user-data directory and trusts only its own record — no mature project kills processes it cannot prove it owns. The rule: the host records the pids of the WebKit processes it creates into its own configuration directory; at startup (before creating any WebKit) it kills only a recorded pid that still names a WebKit process (pid-recycling guard) and whose owning application instance is provably dead; everything else is untouchable, and that boundary is part of the contract test. Acceptance: a reproducible red test (per-exit-path leftovers survive today), the record-and-reap implementation in both hosts, three idempotent start-quit cycles with the leftover count at zero and foreign applications' WebKit untouched, and the V5-109 record corrected. Done on 2026-09-30: both hosts record the pids of the WebKit XPC they create into `webkit-children.json` in their configuration directory (Chromium SingletonLock precedent — own territory, own record) and re-appraise the record on every page load, so WebKit's replacement of a killed child is followed. At startup, before creating any WebKit, a host kills only a recorded child that is alive, still names a WebKit process (recycling guard), and whose start time matches the record, while a live sibling instance (`host_pid` alive and soksak) suspends reaping entirely; the pure decision is a paired contract test in both hosts (`webkit-children.reap.requires-alive-webkit-same-start`). Red: the case matrix (`e2e/webkit-reaping.mjs`, both applications) failed on every assertion — no record existed. Green: all ten assertions pass per application, three idempotent start-quit cycles of the release build hold the count flat, and the unrecorded decoy (a foreign-store WebKit lookalike) survives every path. The matrix covers partial-kill states beyond on/off cycles: an external kill of one child while the host lives (the record must follow the replacement), a crash with one recorded child already dead (the mixed state), and a recorded lookalike victim proving the kill path deterministically without waiting for the rare spawn race. Gates: `pnpm test`, `make docs-check`, `make host-parity-check`, `make native-test`, `make host-contract-check` (229/229) all pass; the V5-109 record is superseded by this entry.
      - [o] V5-114 — P1: Selecting a terminal card briefly focuses the browser card's address bar. User report (2026-09-30, release build, two terminal cards and one browser card): choosing a different terminal card makes the browser address bar take focus for a moment. The mechanism is traced: terminal image regions are click-transparent (`image_region.m` hitTest returns nil), so the press makes the main webview the first responder; the browser address input stays `document.activeElement` by design (nothing ever blurs it), and when the document regains focus WebKit re-fires the DOM focus event at it, which the `browser.address.select` binding amplifies into a visible focus+select flash before the pending-surface-focus flow returns focus to the pressed terminal. The observation contract first (per the user: log on click): page focusin/focusout reports, a card-focus action marker, and a host focus-transition trace line on the timeline the performance trace already owns; then the red reproduction on both entry paths (command and real click), the fix at the measured origin, and green as zero address-focus transitions with no focus-test regressions. Done on 2026-09-30: the origin is fixed in the native press rule. A press inside another image region is now a region-to-region transition — the previously focused region reports its focus loss and hands the first responder to the pressed region in the same event pass, so the page webview never holds the keyboard and the address field's stale active element is never refocused. Red: the native activation test pressed region B while region A held the responder and the responder ended on the page webview (B never reported its gain); the synthetic-pointer e2e path cannot reproduce this — the responder churn is AppKit real-event routing, and the probe built on `input.pointer` was measuring nothing (kept as an end-state regression check with its claim corrected). Green: the activation test passes (loss reported, responder on B, gain reported), the full native suite passes in both tiers, the focus e2e regressions pass, and the observation instruments committed earlier (page focus reports, a card-focus action marker, a host focus trace line) remain for future diagnosis. Gates: `pnpm test`, `make docs-check`, `make native-test` all pass.
      - [o] V5-115 — P1: A card can carry assigned panels on all four sides. The card's left inset sidebar is coupled to the active tab's plugin (its set is re-derived when the tab changes); the user wants panel slots that are assigned once and stay — up to one per side (top, bottom, left, right; four in total), each showing a set chosen explicitly for that card, with the top and bottom panels spanning the card's full width and the coupled sidebar remaining in the center cell. Everything else is the same treatment as the inset sidebar: the section/set system draws it, width/height and fold live in card data, the divider takes drag-to-size and click-to-fold. Acceptance: red tests fail today (no panel exists — the commands are refused and nothing renders); green proves assignment persistence, the decoupling claim directly (switching the card's active tab to another plugin leaves the assigned set unchanged), toggle/size state machine, dangling set ids preserved rather than silently cleared, top/bottom full-width rendering with the surface following the shrunk slot, coexistence with the coupled sidebar, and no regressions in the pinned sidebar geometry tests. Done on 2026-09-30: the panel state is a pure module (`card-panels.js` — sides, assignment, resolve, toggle, size, report) that the plane wires into the card grid, three commands (`core.card.panel.set/toggle/size`), a grip dom exposure, and a `panels` field in the `core.grid` card report. The card content band became a 3×3 grid: top and bottom panels span the card's full width, left and right panels flank the center cell that keeps the inset sidebar and the surface slot, and the surface follows its shrunk slot because the compositor measures the DOM. A native trap was fixed on the way — `grid-row:-1` pins to the last grid *line*, creating an implicit row and shifting the card footer 7 pt; the footer now pins to the last row as `-2 / -1`. Red: the unit red failed on the missing module and the e2e red on the unknown commands. Green: the unit proves assignment persistence, the decoupling claim directly (switching the active tab to another plugin leaves the set unchanged), the toggle/size state machine, dangling set preservation, per-side uniqueness, and the report shape; the e2e proves all four sides on a real card on both hosts (assignment, `core.sidebars` reporting under `cardId:side`, tab-switch invariance, fold/size with the shared 120–480 bounds, off clearing only its side) with the pinned sidebar geometry suites passing unchanged. Gates: `pnpm test`, `make docs-check`, and the sidebar/choices/panels e2e suites on both hosts all pass.
      - [o] V5-116 — P1: The plugin/sidebar model names the card's sides as card sides and permits every placement combination. User concept (2026-09-30, verbatim): a plugin may have a card and four side areas (card-top, card-bottom, card-left, card-right); the four sides group as sidebar material and can connect to any card position (4) and any sidebar (window left/right). Every combination is permitted: a card plugin with no sections; a card plugin with one section; a plugin with no card and sections only (sidebar material only); a plugin with three sections wanting them placed top/bottom/left (layout involvement); a third-party plugin composing other plugins' sections into a sidebar. A card uses as many sides as needed at the positions needed — there is no empty space: the card area uses whatever room the sidebars leave. The rename directive stands inside this: the awkward "rail" wording becomes "card" wording everywhere (settings rows, the link place on the wire, `chooseLink`/`resolveSidebar`, the plane identifiers, the specifications in both languages), the settings choices distinguish window left/right from the card's four sides, and stored `rail` links fail validation as the old shape (no backward compatibility). Sub-items: V5-116-1 the rail→card rename; V5-116-2 the four-side per-plugin placement in settings (the plugin's rows choose sets for card-top/bottom/left/right, giving its cards those panels; an explicit per-card assignment from V5-115 overrides and then persists — 지정만 하고 연동은 끊는다); V5-116-3 verification that every combination is permitted (card-only, card+sections, sections-only, multi-section layout, third-party set composition) and the card area fills the remaining space. Done on 2026-09-30 in three sub-items. V5-116-1 (the rename): the link place is `card-left` on the wire and the settings key is `cardSidebar` — stored `rail` links and keys fail validation as the old shape (no backward compatibility, verified by a rejection test), the settings rows read 카드 사이드바 위치 and 카드 왼쪽 사이드바 beside the window's 왼쪽/오른쪽 rows, and the specifications name the card sidebar accordingly in both languages (the theme's `rail` color token keeps its name — it colors the divider line, not the sidebar place). V5-116-2 (the four-side placement): the link vocabulary gains `card-right`, `card-top`, and `card-bottom` beside `card-left` in `chooseLink`, `resolveSidebar`, and the settings validation; the plugin settings show all four card-side rows; and a card's panel resolves its explicit assignment first, then the plugin's linked set as the default (`effectivePanel`) — turning an explicit assignment off reveals the default again. The card's left side stays the inset sidebar's place: its set comes from the `card-left` link through `cardSidebar`, and a left panel exists only by explicit assignment (a measured collision — both sidebars drew and shifted the surface 190 pt — settled the boundary). V5-116-3 (the combinations): registration accepts a sections-only plugin (sidebar material only), a card-only plugin, a card with one section, and three sections composed by any set including a third party's; the card area fills whatever room the sidebars leave. Green: the unit suite proves the vocabulary rejection, the linked-default precedence (assignment wins, off reveals the default), and the earlier decoupling claims; the e2e suites pass on both hosts for sidebar geometry, choices, settings, and panels; `pnpm test`, `make docs-check` pass. The one collision found on the way (double left sidebar) is recorded above with its measurement.

The shell owner is inactive (`unavailable: true`), but its column remains. Commit `63566222` changed the previous focus-dependent `right` selection into simultaneous `window-right` links for both plugins and inserted separate columns. The two section tabs belong to one browser sidebar, not two content cards. Requiring a presentation choice before this diagnosis was premature; remove that dependency and review the observed column and rail creation against the requested contract before selecting a correction.                   - [o] V5-117-1-3-4-7-1-5 — P0: Deliver the `core.project.open` reply after the library is replaced by the workspace, linked to G2.4. On the release built from `291a39c1` with a new disposable configuration, `core.project.open` opened the project (sidecars started, `host.windows` reports the project window ready, `projects.json` holds one project) but the endpoint reply never arrived; the CLI waited over two minutes and received `main closed (1003)` only when `host.quit` closed the window. Reproduce on the debug build with an owning Red before correcting the reply path. Diagnosis on the debug build from `4e57c27e` (macOS 26.6.2 arm64): the same command stays pending without the check fixture while `host.window` reports `occluded: true` (the window launched behind another application). The page holds the project grid, but the non-activating capture is 99% background color with no page chrome, because `showProject()` awaits `presented()` (drawing, native batch and `host.waitPresented()`) and WebKit does not render an occluded window. Moving the window with `host.window.move` to an uncovered position changes `occluded` to false and the reply arrives at the same moment (49 s after the request). Window checks place windows uncovered through `place()`, so no check covered this path; a new first-open check passes on both debug hosts only in that uncovered placement. Establish owning Red with the window moved off-screen, then decide whether presentation continues while occluded or the command reports an explicit error. Cause: WebKit hides the document of an occluded window and stops animation frames, and the layout queue waits for an animation frame and the next presentation before a command replies. Correction: `sp_window_set_main_webview` disables WebKit window occlusion detection for the app DOM webview (`_setWindowOcclusionDetectionEnabled:`), with an explicit registration failure when the selector is missing; `core.window.document` exposes `visibility` and publishes `visibilitychange`. Red on the unchanged native library: `window_occlusion_test` reports the covered document `hidden` and no animation frame within 5 s. Green: the same test passes and its tracked repeat passes 10/10 without activating the application. On rebuilt debug hosts, a Tauri window behind other applications (`occluded: true`) keeps `visibility: visible`, `core.project.open` replies immediately, and the original-resolution non-activating capture shows the complete workspace. Window checks `the first project opened from the library replies…` and `a covered window keeps its document visible…` pass 4/4 in three runs on both hosts with another application frontmost; the latter failed on the unchanged hosts at `the window became uncovered during the check`, which is recorded as observation, not as the deterministic Red. Workbench 193/193 (macOS 26.6.2 arm64 / Node v26.8.1).
                  - [ ] V5-117-1-3-4-7-1-6 — P1: Keep surface webviews rendering while their window is occluded. V5-117-1-3-4-7-1-5 changes only the app DOM webview; plugin surfaces attached through `webviewAttachSurface` still follow WebKit occlusion, so their non-activating captures and paint acknowledgements may stop while the window is covered. Establish owning Red with an attached surface before correcting it.
                  - [ ] V5-117-1-3-4-7-1-7 — P1: Report a command whose reply never arrives. During V5-117-1-3-4-7-1-5 the CLI waited over two minutes for `core.project.open` without an error, although commands longer than 10 seconds must declare `timeout` and the host relay applies it. Reproduce the missing relay bound in the owning endpoint module before correction. Analysis: the current contract allows this wait. `docs/spec/exposure.md` lets the host wait without a bound for `command.run` handled by the main page, and the contract case `exposure.relay.no-timeout-waits-until-close` verifies it, while declared and 10-second default timeouts apply only to commands forwarded to surface pages. The defect is the contract: a core command without a reply is never reported. Revise the contract so main-page commands also use their declared timeout or the 10-second default, declare `timeout` for core commands that legitimately run longer, and replace the case through owning Red/Green.

The consumed-wait split of the frame timings, the host and page producer sets, and the sampler remain. ## Tauri/Wails parity audit (2026-09-21)

The mechanical host check reports `apps/tauriv2/test/runtime.test.mjs: no counterpart in apps/wailsv3`. This remains a file-structure mismatch. Wails tests `waitPresented` dispatch in `runtime-contract.test.mjs`, and G2.5 now asserts the returned `{ displayed: 42 }` value through both adapters; the missing filename alone does not establish missing behavior coverage.

The bounded diagnostic capture contract is now symmetric for the current macOS hosts. Both `diagnostics.capture.stop` implementations return `frames`, `count`, `limited`, and `longestGap`; the Wails platform adapter reads the shared macOS cap state, and its payload tests cover capped and uncapped results. The shared macOS capture implementation and the recording state tests remain separate from gesture-completeness measurement: `limited: true` is normal, while a gesture whose end was not recorded fails measurement.

At the 2026-09-21 audit snapshot, the modal implementation had a larger confirmed gap. Tauri's `modals::show` created a child native WebView, loaded `/overlay.html`, kept it hidden until `ready`, then applied the mode-specific background, raised it, and focused it. At that snapshot Wails `OverlayShow` only stored modal state and emitted `modal-content`; it did not create or load a modal WebView, and Wails' `ConfigureModal` and `FocusModal` methods had no call site. That Red established an implementation gap, not an appearance-only test failure. The later F10.2 correction creates the Wails child-WebView contract; its current rebuilt-host evidence is recorded in F10.2 and remains subject to the wider F10 gate.

The host test file sets otherwise have matching names, including `recording_test`. The structure checker accumulates differences; it does not stop at that runtime file. Matching names do not establish matching behavior. The previous 27-track source-glob audit omits native bridge languages outside its fixed roots and does not execute behavior checks; its pass is not feature-parity evidence.

The real-session PTY close test failed with `No such process` when closing an already-exited child. After checking and reaping that child explicitly, the same two-session test passes without skipping. Full service reconnect, descendant cleanup, and repeated-close resource measurements remain pending.

## Earlier validation

Terminal keyboard forwarding is corrected on macOS (2026-09-20). Tauri's empty image-event callback discarded native input; the terminal plugin also discarded the character of modified keys in both hosts. Before correction, the three-terminal native-input test failed ordinary typing in Tauri and Ctrl+U in Wails. After correction, both rebuilt hosts passed all eight test cases (two parent cases and six terminal cases), with no failures or skips: native click, character input, Backspace, Ctrl+U, Enter, exactly-once command output, and isolation from the other terminals. `terminal.screen` publishes sidecar screen notifications; checks no longer repeatedly request screen content. Native tests, package tests, both builds, and structural checks passed. These results validate keyboard input, not the remaining composition failures. The originally reported window was preserved.

Current composition verification (2026-09-21, macOS 26.6.2) is green for the rebuilt-host scope recorded here. The baseline Wails build reproduced shell white pixels and terminal boundary intrusion. The cause of the Wails empty-terminal failure was a missing `compositionDeclare` registration and immutable declaration path; the host now rejects undeclared placement and records the declaration before placement. Rebuilt Tauri and Wails runs each passed the three-terminal project-return test and the fixed-cell rapid-divider test. The tests require actual movement, six round trips, three terminal regions in every capture, no white pixels or border intrusion, stable glyph metrics, and restored terminal/session identity. `make -C native/darwin test`, `make docs-check`, and `git diff --check` pass. This does not establish the remaining process-lifecycle, cursor, OSC, IME, or loading requirements.

Current-HEAD confirmation (2026-09-21, macOS 26.6.2): binaries built from `660d176` passed the same three-terminal rapid-divider and project-return test independently in Tauri (1/1, 6.14s) and Wails (1/1, 5.42s). The Wails process log reported `vcs.revision=660d176`. These results establish the two-host composition behavior for that built revision only; they do not close the remaining runtime, cursor, protocol, IME, or restoration requirements.

Contracts: [projects and settings](spec/projects.md), [layout](../packages/soksak/docs/layout.md), [surface placement](spec/native-surfaces.md), [native modals](spec/native-modals.md). Procedures: [build and verify](operations/examples.md).

Library simplification verification on 2026-09-08 rebuilt and restarted both macOS hosts. The full example run passed 37 checks, failed 2 recording checks for insufficient frame counts (Wails 56, Tauri 55; more than 60 required), and skipped 2 display-transition checks because only one 2× display was connected. The two failed checks passed when rerun with the same code and criteria. Frame-count reliability remains intermittent; this change does not correct it. Go 3/3 and Rust 3/3 storage tests and `make docs-check` passed. Windows/Linux native execution is unverified. The unchanged layout library retains its `1a921b4` validation: 310 tests passed and all 142 mutations detected.

Recording completeness (2026-09-17): the capture writes frames on a separate queue and `diagnostics.capture.stop` reports the longest display gap; drag recordings fail above 100ms. Before this, disk writes of up to 480ms at 2× dropped frames and caused the intermittent frame-count failures noted above. Window resize and maximize are measured by `resize.test.mjs`; the layout follows the window frame 250–400ms late during the AppKit or Wails animation (WebKit/WebKit#72971, tauri-apps/tao#1207), and the settled frame matches the window size.
